GOD HATH SPOKEN 


and the government shall be upon his shoulder: and his 
name shall be called Wonderful, Counsellor, The mighty 
God, The Father of Eternity, the Prince of Peace.” Here 
you have the “lowly bush” and the “flame”; the humanity 
and the Deity; His relation to time, and His eternity; His 
lowliness and majesty. Micah 5:2: “But thou, Beth- 
lehem Ephratah, though thou be little among the thousands 
of Judah, yet out of thee shall he come forth unto me that 
is to be ruler in Israel; whose goings forth have been from 
of old, from everlasting.” 

What could be clearer or less ambiguous than this 
declaration of the Godhead of the pre-existent Christ and 
of the manhood which He was, in the set time to assume? 
Note the facts and let the mystery be pushed into their 
light. A great ruler is to be born on earth of the covenant 
nation Israel. He is to be born in Bethlehem, the city of 
David, in the tribe of Judah. It is then affirmed of this 
human to be born that His goings forth have been from 
of old, from everlasting (margin “from the days of 
eternity”). Here we have the “child born” in Bethlehem, 
and the “Son given” Who was from eternity. Matt. 2: 
5, 6 and John 1:46 record the precise fulfillment of this 
wonderful prophecy. 

In Prov. 8:15, 16, we have the eternal Christ as the 
incarnation of wisdom saying: “By me kings reign, and 
princes decree justice. By me princes rule, and nobles, 
even all the judges of the earth,” and in verses 22 and 23: 
“The Lord possessed me in the beginning of his way, 
before his works of old. I was set up from everlasting, 
from the beginning, or ever the earth was.” 

Could these things be predicted of the attributes of 
wisdom? Absurd. If of a person, that One is divine. 


Se ae Pe Re eer 
— 183 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


No creature could employ these terms. When we follow 
the teaching of the Spirit in the Word, these things are 
applied to the only One in the universe of whom they 
could be true. He Who decrees the ruling of princes is 
Himself designated “the prince of the kings of the earth.” 
(Rev. 1:5.) “He Who impersonates wisdom is Christ 
* * * the wisdom of God.” (1 Cor. 1:24.) He Whom 
Jehovah “possessed in the beginning of his way, before 
his works of old” is the One of Whom it is written: “In 
the beginning was the Word and the Word was with God.” 
(John 1:1, 2.) He of Whom it is declared “then I was 
by him as one brought up with him; and I was daily his 
delight, rejoicing always before him” is the same of Whom 
the Father thrice during the Son’s earthly pilgrimage said: 
“This is my beloved Son in whom I am well pleased.” 
(Matt. 3:17; 12:18; 17:5.) In John 1:1-3, we have the 
plainest possible declaration of the pre-existent Godhead 
of Jesus Christ. “In the beginning was the Word” not 
began to be, but existing from everlasting as the divine and 
eternal Son. “The Word was with God.” With Him 
from everlasting before men, or angels, or creation had a 
being. The present tense is the only one Deity can use. 
Hence the explanation of such passages as “Jehovah hath 
said unto me, Thou art my Son; this day have I begotten 
thee.” (Ps. 2:7.) The eternal God has no past and no 
future, only an eternal now. So the “Word was with 
God” before creation, in the virgin’s womb, during His 
earthly ministry, on the cross, in the tomb, at the right 
hand of the Majesty on high—ever was, is and shall be 
with Him. A distinct Person, a divine Person, and ever 
one with the Father. “All things were made by him,” 
thus asserting His creative power and so emphatically 


ie 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


declaring His Deity. He was before all things or He 
could not have created all things. “For in him were all 
things created, in the heavens and upon the earth, things 
visible and things invisible, whether thrones or dominions 
or principalities or powers; all things have been created 
through him, and unto him; and he is before all things, 
and in him all things consist.” (Col. 1:16, 17, R. V.; 
1 Cor. 8:6.) 

How culpable in the light of such Scriptures—for we 
have but culled a few because of space limitation—is it to 
hold a modern modified form of the Socinian heresy that 
Jesus Christ began to exist actually when He was con- 
ceived by the Holy Ghost in the womb of the virgin? 


If these Scriptures do not establish the pre-existent 
Godhead of Jesus our Lord, then language is valueless to 
communicate exact thought! 

Second. Consider briefly sample passages showing the 
relation in which the pre-incarnate Christ subsisted in that 
eternal past. It was a filial relation: He was the ever- 
lasting Son. “Thou art my Son, this day (God’s day is 
eternity) have I begotten thee.” (Ps. 2:7.) As Son His 
“goings forth have been from of old, from everlasting.” 

Just as the great natural sun radiates light and heat 
and life in his goings forth moment by moment, so, as far 
as earthly similitude can suggest the motions of the Infinite 
One, Christ has been everlastingly proceeding from the 
Father and His beneficent and merciful “goings forth” 
“have been from of old, from everlasting.” 

Let us refuse to be satisfied with superficial concep- 
tions of this eternal relationship in which the Lord Jesus 
subsisted with the Father. What do we mean when we 


ee SS ES 
— 185 — 


ce a E E E S R S 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


read or speak of the Son of God? Adam was by creation 
a son of God; angels are styled sons of God. (Job. 38:7.) 
But the Christ is the Son of God. 

The Lord Jesus is called Son of God both in respect 
of His divinity and of His humanity. His eternal Sonship 
is declared as in the bosom of the Father. (John 1:18.) 
He is called Son of God as being born of the virgin. (Luke 
1:35.) He is called such by special decree, as in resur- 
rection. (Ps. 2:7; Acts 13:33; Rom. 1:4.) We cannot 
find out to perfection by searching, the eternal Son. “No 
man knoweth who the Son is, but the Father.” (Luke 
10:22.) There must therefore be no vain reasonings but 
a reverent waiting on God for light if we are to grow in 
the knowledge of the Son, and to have fellowship with 
the Father and the Son. (1 John 1:3.) The prism of 
human understanding can never analyze the light in which 
our triune God dwells. “No man hath seen God at any 
time; the only begotten Son (many very ancient authorities 
read ‘God only begotten’) who is in the bosom of the 
Father, he hath declared him.” (John 1:18.) The word 
“declared” in the Greek is the word from which we get 
our word exegesis, i. e., interpretation, exposition. It occurs 
six times in the New Testament in the following passages: 
Bakel24 2355" John 718 s"Acts 10:8 15.17.1427 eo 
With the exception of John 1:18, the R. V. uniformly 
translates “rehearse.” Wycliffe translates “declared” “He 
hath told out.” Who but a divine Person could be the 
exegesis of God—or manifest, interpret, or reveal Him? 

The verse might be translated: “No one has ever seen 
God: the only begotten Son, he who being in the bosom 
of the Father, he has revealed him.” The bosom of the 
Father is an eternal dwelling place and only a divine Person 


— 186 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


could be eternally there. He is the “Logos,” the Word, the 
expression of the Father. He is the:“image of the in- 
visible God.” Not that He became such after His incarna- 
tion, but prior to all creation He was eternally God’s per- 
sonal expression and manifestation. Hence he who does 
not see God in Christ does not see God at all. (1 John 
2:22, 23; John 14:9.) 

God in the person of Christ has undertaken all redemp- 
tion work for us. The love of God for example is only 
revealed in the Son. (John 3:16; 1 John 4:9, 10.) 

The ultimate proof of the love of God to sinful men 
is in the fact that the “Father sent his son into the world” 
for us, and that “God was in Christ reconciling the world 
unto, himself.” (2 Cor. 5:19.) It, is clear then, that 
Christ was the Son before coming into the world. -Even 
if the statements of those whose “wish is father of the 
thought” respecting alleged incarnations among Hindoos 
and Buddhists were true, it would leave the mystery of the 
incarnate God, Jesus Christ, absolutely unique. But “the 
great mystery of godliness; God manifest in the flesh” 
is fundamental in Christian faith not because of the incarna- 
tion but that He, Who became incarnate, was the ever- 
lasting Son. The doctrine of the eternal Godhead of the 
Son of God is the essential basis of the atonement; and the 
atonement is the only way of reconciliation with an holy 
God. Oh, the depths and the preciousness of this mystery. 
God the Son took upon Him our nature and was born of a 
woman in order that He might accomplish the redemption 
purposed eternally by the triune God. “Wherefore when 
he cometh into the world, he saith: * * *.a body didst 
thou prepare for me; * * * Lo, I am come (in the roll of 
the book it is written of me) to do thy will, O God.” (Heb. 


PARE EARS aS SSS ES Ee ee en rn 
— 187 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


10:5,7,R. V.) Here is a Person distinct from the Father, 
distinct also from that human nature He was to assume and 
to which He refers as “a body prepared.” Who could this 
Person be Who was in the secret counsels of God and Who 
knew it was written in “the roll of the Book” of the divine 
purpose concerning a future coming in the flesh? There 
can be but one answer—Christ, the eternal Son. 


“Who is the liar but he that denieth that Jesus is the 
Christ? This is the Anti-Christ, even he that denieth the 
Father and the Son. Whosoever denteth the Son, the same 
hath not the father.” (1 John 2: 22, 23.) “He that honoreth 
not the Son honoreth not the Father that sent Him.” 
(John 5:23, R. V.) Can the Son be honored even as the 
Father if He is not accorded the same divine honors as a 
member in the Godhead, of equal power, substance and 
glory with the Father and the Holy Spirit? Do we so 
know Him? 


Allow that truth to be obscured and all that is holy 
in God and precious in our salvation is eclipsed. “Who 
loved me and gave himself for me.” (Gal. 2:20.) Who 
was it that so loved me? <A man created on purpose to 
die for me? Nay, verily. It was the Son of God. It was 
not an adopted son or a created Son, but “God so loved 
the world that he gave his only begotten son.” (John 
3:16.) “He spared not his own Son.” (Rom. 8: 32.) 


Blessed be God, we can adopt the language of Scrip- 
ture and say: “We have seen and do testify that the 
Father sent the Son to be the Savior of the world.” (1 
John 4:14.) See in this one epistle from which we have 
just quoted the fulness of the doctrine of the Son of God: 
The blood of the Son cleanseth from all sin. (1 John 1:7.)

The designation “Son of God,” “The Son,” “My well- 
beloved Son,” “The Son of the living God,” “The Son of 
the Blessed,” “The Son of the Highest,” “His dear Son,” 
“One Son (His well-beloved,”) “My Son,” are applied to 
the Lord Jesus Christ one hundred and twenty-eight times 
in the New Testament. 

Testimony is given to the Godhead of the Son: 

By the Father: Psalm 2:7; Matt. 17:5; Heb. 1:8, 
Cf. Ps. 45:6. 

By the Lord Himself: Luke 22:70; John 10:36; 
Jobn 3:13; 17:1; 8:56; 14:9; 10:30.

These and many more. Well may we exclaim with 
the centurion of old: “Truly this was the Son of God.” 
(Mark 15: 39.) 

Third. Note a few among many titles predicable only 
of Deity which are applied to the Lord Jesus Christ. 

(a) Jehovah: Isa. 6:3 with John 12:39-41. Heb. 
12:4, 5 with Gen. 32:24. Compare Exodus 3:14, 15 
with Acts 7 :30-35 and Exodus 20: 1, 2 with Acts 7: 38. 

All the divine appearances of the Old Testament refer 
to one Person. Since God the Father has never appeared 
to man (John 1:18 and 6:46) and since God the Son has 
appeared (1 John 1:1, 2; 1 Tim. 3:16) and since one 
Jehovah is represented as sending another (Zech. 2: 10,11; 
Micah 5:2) and since the Lord Jesus is designated re- 
peatedly as “the sent One,” therefore it is conclusive that 
the Jehovah of the Old Economy is the Lord Jesus of the 
New. 

(b) “The Angel of Jehovah,” “The Angel of the 
Covenant,” “The Angel,” “The Angel sent from God,” 
frequently appearing to men, is Himself designated “God.” 
Note Genesis 16: 7-13 and 18:2-33: in verse 17 one of 
the three angels is called “Jehovah.” The Angel wrestles 
with Jacob (Genesis 32:25) blesses him as only God can 
bless; and in Hosea 12: 3-5, that Angel is called God. In

Surely sufficient proof has been adduced to fully es- 
tablish from the Scriptures the proper Deity of our “Great 
God and Savior Jesus Christ.” Human language has no 
capacity to add weight to this impressive and incontestable 
catena of Scripture passages furnishing divine testimony 
to the true and essential Godhead of the Lord Jesus Christ. 


NN 


— 192 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


This glorious central doctrine of our holy faith is the Pal- 
ladium of the Christian host. Remove it, or ignore and 
dishonor it, and whatever outward form in language or 
rite be preserved, all that is distinctly Christian has gone. 
The church which derogates the glory of Christ as God 
is a temple without the Shekinah Presence; a body with- 
out a Spirit; is Christian only in name, and wears the name 
but to dishonor it. 

What the “Ark of the Covenant” or “Ark of the 
Testimony” was to Israel in their wilderness journey, that, 
and much more the glorious Anti-type, the Lord Jesus, is 
to His church in her pilgrimage. The “Ark” made of 
shittim wood (imperishable) and pure gold (humanity and 
deity) had two aspects: 

(1) The mercy seat, blood-besprinkled, was the throne 
of grace where an holy Jehovah could meet and commune 
with the High Priest, the appointed mediator between God 
and His people. 

Thus signifying the Aaronic High Priesthood of our 
Lord Jesus—who was also the mercy seat (Rom. 3:25; 1 
John 2:1, 2)—who is one Mediator between God and 
man. No man cometh unto the Father but by Him. (John 
14:6.) 

(2) The other aspect of the mercy seat of the Ark of 
the Covenant represented the throne of “the Lord of the 
whole earth.” (Josh. 3:11.) <A throne of righteous Judg- 
ment. (Ps. 96:13; 98:9.) 

To those who by grace through faith are represented 
in the holiest of all by our great High Priest, who, with His 
own blood, appeareth in the presence of God for us, there 
is the experience of blessed communion with the Father 
from above the “blood-besprinkled mercy seat.” But to those 


— 193 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


“who have trodden under foot the Son of God, and have 
counted the blood of the covenant * * * an unholy 
thing. The Lord shall judge. * * *. It is a fearful 
thing to fall into the hands of the living God.” (Heb. 
10: 29-31.) 


As surely as when Israel turned from the Ark of the 
Covenant to “the tabernacle of your Moloch and Chium” 
and Jehovah gave them up “to go into captivity beyond Da- 
mascus” (Amos 5:26-27; Acts 7:43), so surely will the 
church or any part of it which itself forsakes, or has any 
kind of complicity with those who have “trodden under 
foot the Son of God,” or turned from the precious “blood 
of the covenant,” be given up to a captivity more terrible 
and soul-destroying than that of Babylon of old. 


What thoughtful reverent student of holy Scripture 
can contemplate the moral consequences of denying the 
Deity of the Lord Jesus, without a shudder! If Jesus is 
not the eternal Son of God and God the Son, then: 


(a) The Bible is a wholly discredited Book, for every 
promise and every figure, every type and prophecy points to 
Jehovah-Jesus, the Lamb of God, the King of Saints. 


(b) Then the word of the purest, holiest and only sin- 
less life came from the infected fountain of the cruelest 
of lies. 


(c) Then the Lord Jesus was a wretched blasphemer 
(how my heart revolts almost against writing such words 
even for a warning) when He said: “I and my Father are 
one.” “He that cometh from above is above all.” (John 


3:31.)

(d) Then there is no atonement made for our sins.

(e) Then God did not so love the world as to give His

Truly this doctrine of the Godhead of Jesus Christ is 
the article of a standing or a falling church. To one it is

“He that abideth in the doctrine of Christ, he hath

O, church of the living God, arise and purge out this

THE HOLY SPIRIT—HIS PERSON AND 
PURPOSE 


James M. Gray, D. D. 


I have been asked to speak on the Holy Spirit, His 
offices and His work, and first of all it is essential to be 
assured that we have a right to speak of Him in the use of 
the personal pronouns He and Him. 


I. Personality in the popular mind is associated only 
with human beings, and it seems so strange, therefore, to 
speak of the Holy Spirit as a person. 


But lexicography teaches us that personality is simply 
self-conscious and rational existence, or to quote Frederic 
W. Robertson, of Brighton, it is “made up of three attri- 
butes—consciousness, character, and will.” The possession 
of these it is that makes God a person, the angels persons, 
and Satan a person, and most assuredly they may be pred- 
icated of the Holy Spirit. 


First. He has the attributes of a person ascribed to 
Him in Scripture, for His love is spoken of, His truth, His 
holiness, his grace. He can be lied to as Peter tells An- 
anias, and He can be sinned against as our Savior taught 
us, and these things are not conceivable of a mere influence, 
or energy, of the Godhead as some would speak of Him. 


Second. Then also He does the works of a person. 
Creation is His work, regeneration, teaching, comforting. 


a 
— 197 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


He moved holy men of old to write the Scriptures, He is 
the Divine Author of the Bible. He selects the officers of 
the church and qualifies them by His gifts. He works 
miracles. | 

Third. More than this, He not only possesses the at- 
tributes and does the works of a person, but He 1s every- 
where referred to as a person. The word, “Spirit,” itself 
is in the neuter gender to be sure, yet whenever Christ 
speaks of Him in relation either to the Father or Himself, 
it is always in the use of the pronouns, “I,” “Thou,” “He,” 
“Whom the Father will send in my name,” (John 14:26), 
is a well-remembered illustration of this. 

To recur for a moment to His attribute of love, it is 
that more than anything else that finally satisfied my own 
soul as to His personality. His love is spoken of directly 
only once, and that near the close of Paul’s Epistle to the 
Romans where the Apostle beseeches his brethren to pray 
for him, “by our Lord Jesus Christ and for the love of the 
Spirit.” (15:30.) And although the thought there may 
be not so much the love which the Spirit has for us, as the 
love which He inspires im us, yet the former is implied in 
the latter, as it is in that earlier passage in the same epistle 
where it is written that, “the love of God is shed abroad in 
our hearts by the Holy Ghost which has been given unto 
SRE oe See 

To quote an old divine, “When the’ Holy Spirit comes 
into this world and into these souls of ours, it is not merely 
because He is sent by the Father and the Son, but because 
He loves to come. The Father is said to have loved us 
with an everlasting love, but so has the Spirit, The Son is 
said to have rejoiced in the habitable parts of the earth and 
to have had his delights with the sons of men, but the same 


— 198 — 


I eee ONE 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


————_{_[_————————_—__—__[S==SSS———SsSss 


is true of the Spirit. Every fact, every declaration, every 
promise that proves the love of God for us, proves the love 
of the Spirit for us, because the Spirit Himself is God.” 

The*same writer reminds us that His love is shown in 
His names, “the good Spirit,” the “Spirit of Grace,” the 
“Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry, Abba, Father;” the 
“Spirit of liberty,” the “Comforter,” the helper of our 
infirmity. 

It ts shown in the Bible He has written. Love to sin- 
ners breathes like sweet fragrance on every page of it. 

It is shown in His work in our hearts. He comes to 
us; speaks to us; draws us; quickens us; fills us with joy. 
He takes of the things of Christ and shows them unto us, 
and “often when we are praying in our poor imperfect way 
He comes in and lifts us up and kindles the flame within.” 

“O Spirit, beautiful and dread! 
My heart is fit to break 

With love of all thy tenderness 
For us poor sinners’ sake.” (Faber) 

But the evidence of the personality of the Spirit car- 
ries with it, of course, that of His Deity. And yet this is 
separately proven by the relationship He bears to the 
Father and to the Son, since He is definitely called “the 
Spirit of God,” and “the Spirit of Christ.” 

And more especially is it shown in the names He bears 
as expressing His own essential Deity. “The Lord, the 
Spirit,” He is called (2 Cor. 3:18), and “The Eternal 
Spirit” (Heb. 9:14.) 

As Doctor Cumming says, “We need not multiply 
passages, there is but one eternal Being, and He is God. 
The Holy Spirit, Who is eternal, is God.” 

II. Coming now to what may be spoken of as His of- 


Mog 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


fices and work, we find in the first place, that He created the 
world, including man. “The Spirit of God moved upon the 
face of the waters,” with the results recorded in Genesis 
I. “The Spirit of God hath made me,” said Job, “and the 
breath of the Almighty giveth me understanding” (33 :4.) 


Second. He inspired the Bible as already shown. In 
the last words of David, the psalmist said, “The Spirit of 
the Lord spake by me, and His word was in my tongue.” 
(2 Samuel 23:1, 2.) Paul says that the Holy Ghost spake 
by Isaiah in the records of that prophet (Acts 28:25). 
Peter, referring to written prophecy, says that “it came not 
in old time by the will of man: but holy men of God spake 
as they were moved by the Holy Ghost” (2 Peter 1:21). 
The epistles to the seven churches of Asia are concluded in 
each instance by the admonition: “He that hath an ear to 
hear, let him hear what the Spirit saith to the churches.” 


“It is the Spirit’s Bible, 
Copyright every word, 
Only His thoughts are uttered, 
Only His Voice is heard” (Wm. Luff). 


Third. He has had intimate relations with men in all 
the ages. He strove with them in ante-diluvian days seek- 
ing to avert the penalty of the deluge; He gave the wisdom 
to Joseph that preserved the nations from famine in the 
days of the Pharoahs; He furnished the skilled cunning of 
the workmen of the tabernacle; He came upon the elders 
who prophesied in the wilderness of Sinai; He gave Joshua 
his courage to conquer Canaan, and clothed Himself with 
Gideon to overwhelm the Midianites; He hid Elijah from 
his enemies and carried Ezekiel away to the river Chebar; 
He came upon the prophets, and they gave their messages 


=—— 200 — 


, 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


to instruct Israel; He led that people and revived them in 
their spiritual decline. 

It was “not by might nor by power,” but by the Spirit 
that the temple was built after the exile, and the mountain 
before Zerubbabel became a plain. . 

The virgin Mary was found with child of the Holy 
Ghost; Elizabeth and Zachariah were filled with the Holy 
Ghost; Simeon came into the temple by the Holy Ghost. 

Both the Old and the New Testaments abound in ex- 
pressions of this character. 

But let me dwell a moment on the words, “He clothed 
Himself with Gideon,” which is the literal Hebrew of 
Judges 6:34, and which must mean that the Holy Spirit 
entered into the inner man of that great warrior, energizing 
and using him for the miraculous deliverance of his people. 

In I. Chronicles 12:18 we have the converse, when ac- 
cording to the margin, “The Spirit clothed Amasai,” i. e., in 
stead of entering into that captain of the band, he encom- 
passed him, hiding the man himself from sight “and 
causing him to be lost in the manifestation of the power of 
God” (The Eternal Spirit, p. 39). 

As we ponder these wondrous tales, is it not hard to 
restrain from bursting forth in the aspiration of the sacred 


lines : 


“O, for that flame of living fire 
Which shone so bright in saints of old; 
Which bade their souls to heaven aspire, 
Calm in distress, in danger bold!” 

“Send the old-time fire upon us, Lord! 
Send the old-time fire upon us, Lord! 
Send the old-time fire upon us, Lord! 
And burn up all the dross!” 


Fourth. The Holy Spirit sustained peculiar relations 
to Jesus Christ, i. e., to Him considered not as God, but as 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the God-man, the one Mediator between God and man. 
We have already seen that Jesus was conceived by the 
Holy Ghost, not in the sense that our Lord ever called the 
Holy Spirit, Father; but in the sense that He was the me- 
dium or agent through which the conception took place. 


Jesus was anointed by the Holy Spirit at His baptism 
when He entered upon that work of righteousness and re- 
demption as to which He covenanted with His Father from 
all eternity. 


The Holy Spirit led or drove Him into the wilderness 
for that girding of strength as the second Adam, which He 
received in His temptation by Satan. 

Jesus cast out demons by the Spirit of God; He came 
in the power of the Spirit into Galilee; the Spirit of the 
Lord anointed Him to preach the Gospel; through the Holy 
Ghost He gave commandment to the apostles whom He had 
chosen; He baptized with the Spirit; He was full of the 
Spirit Who was given without measure unto Him. 


Through the eternal Spirit Christ offered Himself 
without spot unto God, which has been well characterized 
as the most marvelous declaration of all, teaching as it 
does that Christ’s consent to be crucified as our Redeemer 
was of the Holy Ghost; and also His motive in the great | 
transaction to become an offering unto God; and His spot- 
lessness as a sacrifice for human guilt. 


These are all here ascribed to the presence with Him, 
and in Him, of the Third Person of the Godhead. How 
wonderful it all is, and what a lesson it teaches us as to our 
constant need of the same Spirit in all our Christian life, 
“one holy passion filling all our frame, our hearts an altar 
and His love the flame!” 


— 202 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 
$$ 


Indeed, to go further, it was according to the Spirit of 
holiness that Christ was declared to be the Son of God with 
power by the resurrection from the dead (Romans 1:4). — 

And to quote John’s gospel, it is the same Spirit who 
now glorifies Christ, taking of His things and showing them 
unto us (16:14, 15). 

Fifth. The Holy Spirit bears a peculiar relation to 
the Christian church. 

1. Instrumentally He brought it into existence. 

“Who founded the Christian church?” asks Dr. Philip 
Schaff in his catechism, 

“Our exalted Savior,” he replies, “on the fiftieth day 
after His resurrection, by the outpouring of the Holy Spirit 
upon His disciples at Jerusalem.” 

In the strict sense there was no church until that day, 
and when Christ, at Caeserea Philippi, said, “I will build 
My church,” He had reference primarily to what was 
brought to pass by the Holy Spirit on the day of Pentecost. 

The. material for the building of the church had been 
prepared by and gathered there by the Spirit, for as we 
shall note later, He regenerated the disciples and caused 
them to be of one accord in that place. 

What He did on that day was to take up His abode in 
them, to dwell in them, and as Paul teaches in 1 Corin- 
thians 12:13, to baptize them into one body, that mystical 
body of which Christ is the Head, and of which all we who 
come to Him by faith are the members. 

Thus we all are made to drink of one Spirit, so that it 
is now possible to say, “There is one body and one Spirit” 
(Eph. 4:4). 

2. The Holy Spirit not only brought the church into 
existence, but He maintains it in existence. If there were 


— 203 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


‘no Holy Spirit in the church there would be no church, 
and as a matter of fact a local church that does not rec- 
ognize the presence, the life, the direction in its affairs of 
the Holy Spirit is no church. 

He instrumentally brings souls into it by “convicting 
the world of sin, and of righteousness and of judgment” 
(John 16:7-11). The members of the church are born of 
the Spirit or they are not members at all (John 3:8). 

And when He thus brings them into the church, He 
continues to renew them, and to quicken their mortal bodies 
so that they are “no longer debtors to the flesh to live after 
the flesh” (Rom. 8:12). 

He teaches and comforts them, and brings to their re- 
membrance all things that Christ hath said unto them 
(John 14:26). 

He imparts unto them spiritual gifts (1 Cor. 12:4-11). 
He provides overseers for the church, in other words it is 
His agency by which true bishops, deacons and elders are 
appointed to rule over it. (Acts 20:28). 

It is by Him that the Gospel is preached (1 Peter 
1:12), and through Him that it is obeyed (22). It is in 
Him that believers pray, He helping our infirmity because 
we know not what we should pray for as we ought (Rom- 
ans 8:26; Jude 20). 

III. To sum it all up, especially as to His relation to 
the church, as Dr. James Elder Cumming, in his unique 
work on the subject, says: “The Holy Ghost from the day 
of Pentecost has occupied an entirely new position in the 
redemptive scheme of the Godhead. The whole adminis- 
tration of the church has since then evolved on Him. He 
is the medium through which Christ’s mediatorial work is 
carried on.” 


— 204 — 


CaO S 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 
— 


First. In the first place, everything in the church is 
subject to His will. “All these worketh that one and the 
self-same Spirit, dividing to every man severally as He 
will” (I Cor. 12:11). The reference is to the dispensing 
and distribution of spiritual gifts: “For to one is given 


i by the Spirit the word of wisdom; to another the word of 


knowledge by the same Spirit; to another faith by the same 
Spirit; to another the gifts of healing by the same Spirit; 
to another the working of miracles; to another prophecy; 
to another discerning of spirits; to another divers kinds of 
tongues; to another the interpretation of tongues: * * * 
For as the body is one, and hath many members, and all 
the members of that one body, being many, are one body; 
so also is Christ” (8-12). 


Second. In the second place, everything in the church 
is according to the record of His will, which is found only 
in the holy Scriptures which He caused to be written. 
And this record is now a closed record, it is important to 
remember. The warning of Revelation 22:18, 19 is well 
called “a significant finale.” “If any man shall add unto 
these things, God shall add unto him the plagues that are 
written in this book; and if any man shall take away from 
the words of the book of this prophecy, God shall take 
away his part out of the book of life, and out of the holy 
city, and from the things which are written in this book.” 


Third. In the third place, everything in the church 
is under the actual direction of His will. 


1, This is true of its membership. It is absurd and 
sinful to speak of men joining the church whom He has 
not regenerated, in whom He does not dwell, and whom 
He has not thus baptized into the body of which Christ is 


EE eee eee 
— 205 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the head. Such men do not, and can not join the church 
in the real sense of the word. 

2. This is true of its office-bearers. What authority 
have kings, or prime ministers, or popes, or archbishops, or 
church councils, or pulpit committees or congregational - 
majorities to institute offices in the church other than those 
which the Holy Spirit has instituted; and what authority 
have they to fill any of the offices which He has instituted 
with other men than those whom He has chosen, and on 
whom He has set His seal after patient waiting upon Him 
for the revelation of His will? 

No wonder a recent Episcopal Church congress, in 
New York, should be inquiring how to get rid of clerical 
“misfits.” They had expected much from psychology, the 
report said, but it had failed them. It had done no more 
for them than it had done for the German nation. Their 
requirements, they admitted, were “outgrown, hidebound, 
inelastic, unchangeable and unjust,” and now they had 
come to see that “two features conditioned success, an 
ideal that was worth while, and an ideal that can be 
secured.” 

Where can that ideal be found if not in the Word of 
God, and how is it to be obtained if not through the Spirit 
of God? 

3. This is true of its work. “The Holy Ghost said, 
Separate me Barnabas and Saul for the work whereunto J 
have called them;” “so they being sent forth by the Holy 
Ghost, departed unto Seleucia.” (Acts 13:2, 4.) “It seemed 
good to the Holy Ghost, and to us, to lay upon you no 
greater burden than these necessary things,” said James to 
the Gentile churches in Antioch, Syria and Cilicia (Acts 
15). “They were forbidden of the Holy Ghost to preach

GOD HATH SPOKEN

the Word in Asia,” and when “they assayed to go into 
Bithynia, the Spirit suffered them not.” (Acts 16:6, 7.) 

4. This is true of its worship. Its preaching for ex- 
ample. The reason Paul could return thanks to God for 
“the work of faith and labor of love and patience of hope” 
in the Thessalonian church, was because his Gospel had 
come to them not in word only, but “in the Holy Ghost’ 
(1 Thess. 1:5). Peter assures his Asiatic readers that 
the Gospel had been preached to them “with the Holy 
Ghost sent down from heaven.” 

Native talent, learning, mental discipline are neces- 
sary and desirable, but the greatest pulpiteer trusting only 
in them, is vastly inferior to the simplest expounder of the 
Bible who is anointed by the Spirit of God. 

5. Its prayer as well as its preaching. I do not now 
speak of prayer in the pulpit, but in the prayer-meeting 
where the body of saints meet together supposedly to “con- 
fess their faults one to another, and to pray one for an- 
other that they may be healed” (James 5:16). 

How shall the prayer-meeting be conducted? Shall 
the program be arranged in advance, and during the prog- 
ress of the meeting, shall the leader call out brother so- 
and-so to exhort and brother so-and-so to pray? Or, 
“shall the pressure of the human hand in the assembly be 
withheld in order that the Holy Spirit may have His way?” 

6. Its singing as well as its praying. Dr. A. J. Gordon, 
of blessed memory, was right when he said that an un- 
regenerate person is disqualified from ministering in the 
service of song in God’s house. “We should hardly know 
how to name the custom,” said he, “which has brought a 
sorer blight upon the life of the church, or the heavier re- 
pression upon its spiritual energy than the habit, now so 


I ec a a S

general, of introducing unsanctified, unconverted, and even 
notoriously wicked persons into church choirs.” 

Indeed, covering the whole subject of the Holy Spirit’s 
administration of the church, the same witness says: “If 
one desires the clue to the great apostasy whose dark eclipse 
now covers two-thirds of nominal Christendom, here it is— 
the rule and authority of the Holy Spirit ignored in the 
church, and the servants of the house assuming mastery, 
encroaching more and more on the prerogatives of the 
Head.” 

IV. In my rapid survey of this vast subject I am left 
but little time to dwell on the practical application of it all 
to the individual believer, which calls for a separate 
address, 

But I am reminded by another, that there are two 
things revealed in the New Testament as to the work of 
the Holy Spirit which we nowhere find ascribed to Him in 
the Old Testament. 

One is the administration of the affairs of the church, 
and the other His dwelling in men for the purpose of sanc- 
tifying them. 

On the first of these we have dwelt sufficiently per- 
haps, but the second requires an additional word. 

The believer is sanctified by the indwelling of the 
Holy Spirit from the moment that he receives Christ by 
faith, that is, he is then set apart for God eternally. 

But sanctification is a process as well as an act. There 
is such a thing as growth in grace, in other words, nor is it 
necessarily so slow and gradual an experience as some are 
willing to suppose. It may be realized by leaps and 
bounds. 

That is to say there are crises in the lives of some be-

J 
q

GOD HATH SPOKEN 


lievers suggesting a kind of second Pentecost. Crises 
which may be expected and which should be sought for by 
every one of us. 

These crises are associated with the filling of the Spirit 
to which the New Testament so frequently refers. There 
is but one indwelling of the Spirit, which, as I understand 
the Scriptures, is practically identical with His baptism of 
us into the body of Christ, but there may be many 
“fillings.” 

The filling comes when there is special need for it and 
special yearning to receive it, and as often as the need 
exists and the yearning is expressed. 

In the fourth chapter of The Acts, some of the same 
disciples evidently, who were filled on the day of Pente- 
cost, were again filled; and as a result they spake the Word 
of God with boldness, and were of one heart and one 
mind, and with great power gave witness to the resurrec- 
tion of Christ, and great grace was upon them all. 

Paul urges the Ephesian Christians to “be not drunk 
with wine wherein is excess, but to be filled with the 
Spirit.” It is the secret, he goes on to say, of gladness, of 
gratitude, of graciousness one to another, and it permeates 
with blessing the everyday life in the conjugal relation, in 
the parental relation, and in the industrial relation, and 
thus throughout every class of the social order. 

It is a sin for a Christian not to be filled with the Holy 
Spirit. With a shock that conviction came to me one day 
not so very long ago, and I thank God for it. 

How may we Christians be filled with the Holy Spirit? 
Is any further answer required than that of Christ in the 
eleventh chapter of Luke? “If ye then, being evil, know 
how to give good gifts unto your children; how much more 


— 209 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


shall your heavenly Father give the Holy Spirit to them 
that ask him?” 

But asking presupposes a felt need and a desire. It 
presupposes a surrender of our will. It presupposes an 
obedient life. It presupposes a readiness of faith to accept 
the fulfillment of a divine promise. 

Would to God that this might be true today of every 
member of this Confererice. Would to God that this Con- 
ference might thus mark a renewal and revival of the work 
and the life of God in the church of Christ that would 
kindle fires in every part of the world; that would send 
men and women to their knees in supplication for the mercy 
and the grace of God; that would quicken the testimony of 
the faithful church; that would awaken a new interest in 
the study of the sacred oracles; that would stimulate mis- 
sionary endeavor; that would stop the mouths of gainsayers, 
and save souls, and bring nearer the glad hour when 


An unearthly glory shall steal across the sky, 
And the marshalling of His myriads 
Announce that Christ is nigh. 

Our hearts beat high, Lord Jesus, 

And our glad spirits rise; 

Divide! ye starry heavens, 

Reveal Him to our eyes. 


— 210 — 


SATAN—HIS PERSON, POWER AND PURPOSE 
GEorGE E. GUILLE 


Bible Teacher and Author, Member of the Moody Bible Institute 
Extension Department 


Satan is a fallen creature of God who, transformed 
into an angel of light, is “the prince of this world” and 
“the god of this age,” and to whose activities in the affairs 
of men are due the present state of those affairs, the 
appalling apostasy in Christendom the rise and growth 
of false religious systems, and, indeed, many of the world- 
movements that are attributed to God. 

Let it be understood that I do not speak of the devil 
of Christendom, that mystical monster of the hoofs and 
horns with whom we have become familiar through car- 
toons and caricatures, but concerning whom there is not 
a line in the Word of God. I speak of the Satan whom 
Scripture reveals, a subject of divine revelation alone, of 
which we can know nothing apart from the Bible. 


His Personality 

The personality of Satan is witnessed to everywhere 
in Scripture. 

First. Scripture shows us that he possesses personal 
characteristics. We read of his “wiles” in Eph. 6:11, and’ 
of his “devices” in 2 Cor. 2:11, and that he “deceiveth 
the whole world” (Rev. 12:9)—a very subtile person, 
indeed, and one of remarkable intelligence. We read of 
them who “are taken captive of him at his will.” Now 
a person is a self-conscious being with a free will and we 


Helpful books by the above author listed on pages 446-449, 


elas ll eels ea i ce 
—211— 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


read of this person saying in his pride, “I will.” “I will 
be like the most High.” 

Second. Scripture testifies to his personality in 
ascribing acts of intelligence to him. He “beguiled” Eve 
by his subtilty (2 Cor. 11:3). He “tempted” the Son 
of God. He “demanded” to have the disciples that he 
might sift them as wheat (Luke 22:31). He “accuses” 
the brethren (Rev. 12:10). And the coming of the 
man of sin is declared to be “after the ‘working’ of Satan.’” 
These things could not be said of a merely impersonal 
influence. 

Third. Scripture reveals his personality in the per- 
sonal names that it gives him. He is called “Beelzebub,” 
the prince of demons. He is called “Satan.” He is called 
“the pride of this world” and the “prince of the powers 
of the air.” 


His Pristine Glory 


When we turn to the Scriptures that tell of his 
origin, we find that he was not created as Satan and the 
Devil but as the noblest of all God’s creatures. This is 
disclosed in the 28th chapter of Ezekiel. That chapter 
opens with an address to the prince of Tyrus, who appears 
as a figure of the Man of sin. At verse 11 the prince 
disappears and the address becomes a lamentation upon 
“the king of Tyrus,” the mighty spirit who energizes that 
prince. 

“Son of man, take up a lamentation upon the king 
of Tyrus, and say unto him, Thus saith the Lord GOD; 
Thou sealest up the sum, full of wisdom, and perfect in 
beauty. Thou hast been in Eden the garden of God; every 
precious stone was thy covering, the sardius, topaz, and 


— 212 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the diamond, the beryl, the onyx, and the jasper, the sap- 
phire, the emerald and the carbuncle, and gold; the work- 
manship of thy tabrets and of thy pipes was prepared in 
thee in the day that thou wast created. Thou art the 
anointed cherub that covereth; and I have set thee so; 
thou wast upon the holy mountain of God; thou hast 
walked up and down in the midst of the stones of fire. 
Thou wast perfect in thy ways from the day thou wast 
created, till iniquity was found in thee. By the multitude 
of thy merchandise they have filled the midst of thee with 
violence, and thou hast sinned: therefore I will cast thee 
as profane out of the mountain of God: and I will destroy 
thee, O covering cherub, from the midst of the stones 
of fire. Thine heart was lifted up because of thy beauty, 
thou hast corrupted thy wisdom by reason of thy bright- 
ness: I will cast thee to the ground, I will lay thee before 
kings, that they may behold thee. Thou hast defiled thy 
sanctuaries by the multitude of thine iniquities, by the 
iniquity of thy traffick; therefore will I bring forth a fire 
from the midst of thee, it shall devour thee, and I will 
bring thee to ashes upon the earth in the sight of all them 
that behold thee.” 

Of no earthly king nor of any human being could the 
words uttered here be true. The king of Tyrus is a title 
of Satan whose full significance may appear in the future 
when the man of sin, as “the prince of Tyrus” makes his 
debut. 

As thus created by God, Satan was the most exalted 
of His creatures, the very sum of creative perfection, full 
of wisdom and perfectly beautiful to look upon. 

Eden, the garden of God, and the holy mountain of 
God were the places of his abode. The Eden of earth, 


— 213 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


mentioned in Genesis 2 as already existing in the primal 
creation, before the cataclysm that wrought the chaos de- 
scribed in the second verse of the Bible, was evidently a 
counterpart of the heavenly. “The holy mountain,” we 
learn from other Scriptures, is the place of God’s mani- 
festation and the symbol of His government. This earth, 
before the six days’ work that made it an habitation for 
man, was placed in Satan’s hands. 


The precious stones of his covering we find in the 
breastplate of the high priest as the expression of highest 
excellence. 


The cherubim are associated with the throne of God. 
In the ark of the covenant, they are “of a piece with the 
mercy seat,” and the mercy seat was Jehovah’s throne in 
the midst of Israel. Satan was “the anointed covering 
cherub,” set in that place by God as the guardian of His 
throne. 


But in this perfect being, iniquity was found, and 
that iniquity was a revolt against God. Pride in his posi- 
tion, pride in his beauty led him into an unholy traffic 
among other intelligences of heaven to draw them from 
their allegiance to God. This was his sin: his will in 
rebellion against God, and this is the essence of sin still. 
In Isaiah 14 where we hear him addressed as “Lucifer, 
son of the morning,” the pride of his heart is laid bare: 
“For thou hast said in thine heart, I will ascend into 
heaven, I will exalt my throne above the stars of God. 
* * * JT will be like the most High.” And the Apostle, 
instructing Timothy concerning the ordaining of elders, 
said, “Not a novice, lest being lifted up with pride he 
fall into the crime of the devil.” 


ae a 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


By this very ambition that caused his own ruin, he 
beguiled and dragged down to ruin the head of the human 
race, and the whole race in him. 

His Dignity 

The dignity of Satan is another fact of divine revela- 
tion that appears to have been overlooked by many. 

The Apostle Jude, foretelling the activities ‘of the 
false teachers of the last days, says that they “speak evil 
of dignities” and adds: “Yet Michael the archangel, when 
contending with the devil he disputed about the body of 
Moses, durst not bring against him a railing accusation, 
but said, The Lord rebuke thee.” The highest of the 
angels, recognizing the dignity conferred upon that being 
who, by his fall, has become the Devil, will not rebuke 
him, but appeals to Jehovah to do so. A similar rebuke 
is administered in Zech. 3 where Satan attempts to resist 
the high priest: “And Jehovah said unto Satan, Jehovah 
rebuke thee, O Satan; even Jehovah that hath chosen 
Jerusalem rebuke thee.” 

When the sons of God—a phrase in the Old Testa- 
ment always denoting angels—presented themselves before 
Jehovah, and “Satan came also among them” (Job 1:6), 
Jehovah did not scorn him, but conversed with him about 
His servant Job. 

The titles conceded to him in Scripture prove his 
dignity. Thrice in John’s gospel does our Lord designate 
him as “the prince of this world,” and in 2 Cor. 4:4 the 
Apostle calls him “the god of this age.” 

‘How striking the contrast between this revealed dig- 
nity of Satan and the levity so commonly indulged in in 
speaking of him, the nicknames applied to him and 


ID (ail 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the grotesque misrepresentations with which we are so 
familiar: the leering, disgusting monster, haunting the 
dens of iniquity and inciting men to vice and crime. The 
devil of Christendom is a myth, a scapegoat created by 
human fancy to account for the sins of the world, and I 
do not wonder that so many do not believe in his exist- 
ence. Responsibility for such a conception of Satan can- 
not be laid at the door of Scripture, for it contains no 
word that could give rise to such a notion. What Scrip- 
ture declares of him, on the contrary, is that he is “trans- 
formed into an angel of light.” 


His Relation to the Earth and the World 


Satan is not in hell nor has he ever been there. The 
belief that he presides over the destinies of unregenerate 
men rests upon no Scriptural foundation. The hell of 
eternal punishment has not been opened to receive its 
victims, and when Satan is finally shut up in the bottom- 
less pit it shall not be as an exalted monarch but as the 
most abject creature there. 

Not hell, but the earth and the air are the scene of 
his activity. When Jehovah, in Job 1:6, 7, asked him 
whence he came, he replied: “From going to and fro in 
the earth, and from walking up and down in it,” and, 
although he is a liar, that he told the truth in that instance 
is proven by what Peter says of him in 5:8 of his first 
epistle: “Your adversary, the devil, as a roaring lion, 
walketh about, seeking whom he may devour.” And in 
Rev. 2:13 we hear the Son of God saying to the church 
in Pergamos, “I know where thou dwellest, even where 
Satan’s throne is.” Satan’s throne, then, is on the earth, 
for Pergamos was certainly nowhere else. Pergamos re- 


SS a SRN TBA EN RS RA E A E ESTERS SS 


— 216 — 


a 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


es 


veals the sad state into which the church has fallen: instead 
of being true to her pilgrim character, she has settled down 
as a dweller in a scene where Satan reigns. 

And not only has he a throne in the earth, but he 
claims the kingdoms of the world as his own. Having 
taken our Lord up into an high mountain, he “shewed 
unto him all the kingdoms of the world in a moment of 
time. And the Devil said unto him: All this power will 
I give thee, and the glory of them: for that is delivered 
. unto me; and to whomsoever I will I give it” (Luke 4:5, 
6). And this was not the raving of insanity but a claim 
divinely conceded in the title applied to him by the Son 
of God: “The Prince of this world.” Surely if the claim 
were extravagant or false the Lord would have refuted 
it when it was made. 

The Apostle, in enjoining the saints to put on the 
whole armor of God that they may be able to stand against 
the wiles of the devil, says that our conflict is “with prin- 
cipalities and powers” and the “world-rulers of this dark- 
ness” over whom Satan is the head, as “the prince of the 
powers of the air,’ and a hint of the power he exercises 
through these world-rulers is given in the angel’s word 
to Daniel in explanation of the three weeks’ delay in his 
coming to explain Daniel’s vision: “But the prince of 
the kingdom of Persia withstood me one and twenty days.” 
From this explanation it appears that one of these world- 
rulers of Satan was presiding over the affairs of the 
ancient Persian Empire. 

And not only is this true: the Apostle, in Rom. 8:20 
speaks of one who has subjected the very creation. “For 
the creation was subjected to vanity, not of its own will, 
but by reason of him who subjected it.” (R. V.) This 


— 217 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


can be none other than Satan who subjected creation’s 
lord, Adam, and in him all his posterity, for the Apostle 
John declares that “the whole world lieth in the evil one” 
(1: John’ oel RTVA) 


But thanks be unto God that, though Satan rules as 
“prince of the power of the air” and the “prince of this 
world,” his authority and powers are not absolute, for a 
restraint has been laid upon him. “The mystery of in- 
iquity’—Satan’s travesty on “the mystery of Godliness”’— 
was already at work in Paul’s day, “only,” he tells us, 
“he who now restraineth will restrain, until he be taken 
out of the way” (2 Thess. 2:7). The Holy Spirit, resi- 
dent in the church, thus keeps back the manifestation of 
the Man of sin until the time of the church’s removal 
from the world. 


If the term “anointed,” applied to Satan in his primal 
glory, suggests his offices, it is very significant. In Old 
Testament times men were anointed for three offices: 
prophet, priest and king. Moses foretold, in Deut. 18:15, 
of a Prophet that the Lord God should raise up from the 
midst of Israel, and the Lord Jesus fulfilled that predic- 
tion. A prophet is one who speaks for God, and in John 
17 we hear this Prophet rendering account to God: “I 
have given them the words thou gavest me. * * * And 
now come I to thee.” He enters now the priestly office. 
Received in the world as a Prophet, but rejected as King, 
He now appears in the presence of God as the great High 
Priest of our confession. It is at His second coming that 
He shall appear as King. 


Satan, then, who defiled his sanctuaries, has been 
deposed as prophet and priest, but has not yet been de- 


— 218 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


posed as prince, mor shall be until Christ comes to His own 
as King of kings. 

All this is typified in the history covered by the first 
book of Samuel. David, the anointed of Jehovah, was 
cast out, while: Saul still sat upon the throne. But that 
throne and its occupant lay under sentence of judgment 
from God. And, while a company was secretly gathered 
to David to share his rejection in the cave Adullam—rep- 
resenting the heavenly company being now gathered to 
Christ—it was not until Saul’s overthrow that David was 
crowned as king over all Israel. 


The Sphere of His Activities 


Not a single instance of Satan’s activity is recorded 
in Scripture that does not show him as a trespasser on 
God’s ground. In Eden his sole purpose was to alienate 
man from God. He began his work there by insinuating 
a doubt as to the Word of God: “Yea, hath God said?” 
And succeeding in that, he came boldly out with his lie 
about the penalty of sin: “Ye shall not surely die.” 

In his efforts with Job, he sought to make him dis- 
trust God. He had for long been at work with Job to 
puff him up with self-righteousness, so that when his 
calamity should come upon him he would think that God 
had been unjust with him. It is plain that his stroke was 
not aimed at Job but at God Himself. 

In these, and in other examples of his activity that 
might be named, there is no suggestion of immorality. 
Morals are not the sphere in which he works. Having 
accomplished man’s ruin in Eden, he has not found it 
necessary to tempt him to evil, for evil is the natural 
product of his corrupt heart, as the Lord declares in Mark 


+ 


— 219 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


7:21-23: “For from within, out of the heart of man, 
proceed evil thoughts, adulteries, fornications. * * * All 
these evil things come from within”; and James says that 
“every man is tempted when he is drawn away of his own 
lust, and enticed” (Jas. 1:14). Sins are “the works of 
the devil,” indeed, and it is written that “he that sinneth 
is of the devil.” But the Scripture definition of sin is 
“lawlessness” (1 John 3:4, R. V.). Its very essence is 
the revolt against God. Immorality is incidental. 

There are “unclean spirits” and Satan is their prince, 
and demoniacal possession may account for abnormal 
phases of depravity in men today. But not all demons are 
unclean, for the teachings of demons, against which we 
are warned in 1 Tim. 4:6 as a mark of the last days, 
inculcate the highest morality. 

But is not Satan denounced as a liar and a murderer? 
Yes, but what is the lie, and what is the murder? The 
murder is that of the Son of God, outcome of the lie of 
which Satan is the father. 

Hear the Lord’s words: “Ye are of your father the 
devil, and the lusts of your father it is your will to do. 
He was a murderer from the beginning, and standeth not 
in the truth, because there is no truth in him. When he 
speaketh the lie, he speaketh of his own: for he is a liar, 
and the father thereof.” He is the father of the lie about 
the Son of God. 

To whom did the Lord utter such scathing words? 
“Mark it well. Not to publicans and sinners; not to men 
of corrupt minds and perverted morals, but to the best 
men of the day, men of blameless morality and of the 
greatest devotion to religion. They believed the Devil’s 
gospel and resented the gospel of grace as an impertinence, 


— 220 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


rejecting the Christ of God and nailing Him to the cross. 
These are the men and women who have “gone to the 
devil”: not the social outcast and the moral leper but those 
who are steeped in self-righteousness and religiousness, 
embracing false theologies and false religions while they 
reject the only Lord and Savior, Jesus Christ. 

Religion, not morals, is the domain of Satan’s influ- 
ence. And by religion I do not mean our holy Christian- 
ity, but the religion of Christendom, of which Satan, as 
“the god of this age,” is the author. It is he who has 
' sowed the tares in the wheat field. It is he who has cor- 
rupted the truth with leaven. And so subtile is his trav- 
esty that thousands have mistaken the religion of Christen- 
dom for Christianity, in total ignorance of the fact that 
they are as wide apart as heaven and the bottomless pit. 

No, Satan is not the enemy of morals but of faith. 
An angel of light would scarcely incite men to vice. When 
he demanded to have the disciples that he might sift them 
as wheat, he sought, in the case of Peter, to break down 
his faith, as the Lord makes clear in His prayer for him: 
“I have prayed for thee, that thy faith fail not.” (Luke 
22:32) Elymas, the sorcerer, was denounced as a child 
of the devil because he sought, not to lead the proconsul 
into sin but, to turn him from the faith. (Acts 13:8.) 
And in Peter’s admonition concerning the Devil, he shows 
that it is faith he seeks to pervert, for he says: “Whom 
resist stedfast in the faith.” 

His Aim 
‘And this explains the real aim of Satan. It is to 


obscure, by the admixture of error, or to flatly deny, the 
truth about Christ and to turn men away from Him. 


— 221 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


When God made man in His own image, Satan, by 
his lie, destroyed that image. There is one only way by 
which it can be restored, and that is by having Christ, 
“who is the image of God,” dwell in the heart by faith. 
Satan, knows this far better than some zealous men do, 
and it is what he is so busy to circumvent. Thus is it 
written in 2 Cor. 4:3, 4: “But if our gospel be hid, it 
is hid to them that are lost: In whom the god of this 
age hath blinded the minds of them which believe not, lest 
the light of the glorious gospel of Christ, who is the 
image of God, should shine unto them.” This, as the 
late Sir Robt. Anderson said, is why men turn to the 
church, to religion, to the sermon on the mount, to any- 
thing and everything except the cross of Christ. Satan 
will lead men into the highest walks of morality and 
benevolence and religion in order to blind them to ‘their 
needs of Jesus Christ, and the Satan cult is found in the 
religious systems that honor men and dishonor Christ and 
that accept the Eden gospel of lies: “Ye shall not surely 
die,” “Ye shall be as God.” “The new gospel” this is 
called. There is nothing new in it. It is as old as its 
father, the Devil. 

Working in this sphere, Satan has robbed the church 
of her blessed hope—the coming of her Lord—and she is 
in many quarters forsaking her divine commission to preach 
the gospel and bring sinners to Christ, and has gone at 
world-mending, trying to patch up this ruined world and 
restore paradise without Christ. Social service has be- 
come her program. Mend a world that God has judged! 
Improve a scene that lieth in the wicked one! Social 
service is right in its place, but when it displaces Jesus 
Christ it is devilish. 


— 222 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


His Methods 


The people of God have nothing to fear from Satan’s 
power, but they are warned against his “wiles” and his 
“devices.” And these terms reveal his methods. He ac- 
complishes his ends by guile and deception. “The serpent 
beguiled me,” said Eve, and the Apostle confirms this 
when he says, “I fear, lest by any means, as the serpent 
beguiled Eve through his subtilty, so your minds should 
be corrupted from the simplicity that is in Christ.” 

Where he is seen in Rev. 12:9, he is “called the devil 
and Satan, which deceiveth the whole world.” And we 
are told that the purpose in shutting him up in the bottom- 
less pit is “that he should deceive the nations no more.” 

His deception is often by imitation. He has his syna- 
gogues (Rev. 2:9) and his ministers. Concerning these 
latter, hear the Apostle: “And no marvel; for Satan him- 
self is transformed into an angel of light. Therefore it 
is no great thing if his ministers also be transformed as 
the ministers of righteousness.” And who are these? I 
will tell you who they are. They are scholarly gentlemen 
of gracious and pleasing personality, and of charming and 
eloquent speech, who stand in Christian pulpits and deny 
the deity of our Lord and the other fundamentals of our 
Christian faith. They all preach righteousness, but not 
one of them preaches grace. 

“Modernism” is the product of Satan’s lie. The world 
achieved its boasted wisdom in listening to that lie in 
Eden, accepting, at his suggestion, the pursuit of knowl- 
edge as its career—‘“ye shall know’—and in that fatal 
choice forfeiting all right to that “hidden wisdom which 
God ordained before the world unto our glory.” And 
now the modern rationalism, exalting human reason above 


NEE EES EE OOS Ae 
| — 223 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


all things in its effort to do away with supernaturalism, 
humanizes God and deifies man. It is Satan’s stroke 
against the throne and the heart of God. How shall we 
account for the fact that the great institutions committed 
to “Modernism” are rolling in wealth while those that 
remain steadfast in their fidelity to the Word of God still 
struggle with poverty? It is the predicted apostasy, the 
working of the mystery of iniquity. But in it all, Satan’s 
devices appear, for “a form of godliness” is retained, while 
the power thereof is denied. 

The popular literature of the day teems with Satan’s 
gospel and many a magazine article contains a veiled stab 
at the infinite heart of Christ. 

But it is in the spread of false religious systems that 
Satan holds high carnival. Such are Christian Science, 
Russellism, Seventh Day Adventism, Theosophy, Spiritism 
and all the others. 

His masterpiece appears in Christian Science, falsely 
so-called. That such a complicated fabrication of blas- 
phemies and false philosophy. set forth in such an inco- 
herent jumble of words, can delude intellectual men and 
women proves to any spiritual mind that a mighty and 
evil spirit is working in it. It comes to us in the name 
of Christ yet it denies Him; denies His personality, denies 
His deity, denies His death and resurrection as atonement 
for sin, denies His exaltation and His return. It denies 
the very existence of sin—the most obvious fact of human 
history and experience, declaring that man is incapable of 
sin. It blasphemes the Holy Spirit by declaring Him to 
be Christian Science. It denies the judgment and the 
eternal punishment of sinners. 

While declaring the non-existence of sickness, it pro- 


— 224 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


poses to heal the body. That it does so, I have no doubt, 
for Satan must have the same power to heal that he has 
to afflict. Phenomena may be superhuman without being 
at all divine: they may be angelic or demoniacal or satanic. 
The text-book of this Satan cult bears a significant title: 
“Science and Health,” for Satan told the truth concerning 
the mass of mankind when he said of Job, “All that a 
man hath will he give for his life.” Men will sell their 
souls to the devil to obtain relief from pain. 

Of those who have gone out of Christian churches 
into this awful delusion, it is written in 1 John 2:19, 
“They went out from us, but there were not of us; for 
if they had been of us, they would have continued with 
us: but they went out, that they might be made manifest 
that they were not all of us.” Do not be disturbed, then, 
when you see others forsaking your church for Eddyism. 
If they continue in it, it only proves that they were never 
saved. If they return from it to your fellowship again, 
it proves that they are the children of God who have been 
temporarily deluded. 

Another of Satan’s successful devices is Russellism, 
a system containing some truth, but with such an admix- 
ture of error as to make of it a tremendous peril. Its 
attraction for the sinner is its denial of punishment and its 
doctrine of a second chance. Its attraction for the Chris- 
tran is its false exposition of prophecy. 

In the modern Spiritism and Theosophy Satan is 
working through demons to deceive thousands. The phe- 
nomena of Spiritism cannot be accounted for on merely 
human grounds. These phenomena are real, but they are 
the working of demons taking possession of human bodies 
through which they almost exclusively manifest themselves. 


— 225 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


From the ouija board to the seance, all is of the Devil. 
Let every child of God avoid it, for God has everywhere 
forbidden it. 


His Ultimate Purpose 


The ultimate purpose of Satan is the manifestation 
of the Man of sin. Through all these present agencies he 
is busily preparing the world to believe his lie and to 
receive that Lawless One, of whom it is written: “For 
the mystery of iniquity doth already work: only he who 
now restraineth will restrain, until he be taken out of the 
way. And then shall that Wicked be revealed, whom 
the Lord shall consume with the spirit of his mouth, and 
shall destroy with the brightness of his coming: Even 
him, whose coming is after the working of Satan with all 
power and signs and lying wonders, And with all deceiv- 
ableness of unrighteousness in them that perish: because 
they received not the love of the truth, that they might 
be saved. And for this cause God shall send them strong 
delusion, that they should believe the lie.” 

Believe what lie? The lie that Satan began in Eden: 
“Ye shall not surely die.” It is the denial of punishment. 
And that lie is a tenet of nearly all the false religions on 
earth. 


His Doom 


For a little while during the great tribulation, and 
after the church has been caught away to be with the Lord, 
Satan shall be allowed to have his way without restraint 
while God’s judgments are being executed, but his doom 
is clearly revealed and, thank God, it is almost at hand. 
The Lord Jesus, whose heel he bruised at the cross, shall 
come forth in power and glory to bruise his head, and to 


— 226 — 


nee ee ee 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


fulfill what is written in Rev. 20:1-3: “And I saw an angel 
. come down from heaven, having the key of the bottom- 
less pit and a great chain in his hand. And he laid hold 
on the dragon, that old serpent, which is the Devil, and 
Satan, and bound him a thousand years. And cast him 
into the bottomless pit, and shut him up, and set a seal 
upon him, that he should deceive the nations no more, till 
the thousand years should be fulfilled.” 

And though, at the end of that period, he shall be 
loosed, it is but for “a little season,’ and “the lake of 
fire,” “prepared for the devil and his angels” shall at last 
receive him. 

Let the Christian abide in Him who has overcome 
Satan, “and that wicked one toucheth him not,” and so let 
him rejoice in that promise of Rom. 16:20: “The God 
of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly.”

SIN—ITS NATURE, EXTENT AND EFFECT 


JoserH Kyte, D.D., LL.D. 


Christian brethren, tonight I am to deal with the 
most appalling fact to be discovered in all the vast range 
of the universe of God; a fact that holds within its awful 
compass the sum of all the woes. Out of it, as from a 
seething Stygian pool, all evils flow; and back to it, as 
to a bottomless pit, they all return: for in that strangest 
phrase that ever was spoken by our Lord, there is the 
revelation of the very essence of Hell’s despair, “guilty 
of an eternal sin,” that argues an eternal Hell. Certain 
deluded brethren are once more advancing the baseless 
theory of “conditional immortality” and its corallary the 
“annihilation of the wicked.” Here is a phrase that dis- 
covers the absolute unscripturalness of the conception. 
In the very nature of things an eternal sin predicates 
eternal existence, eternal consciousness, eternal respon- 
sibility, and cannot mean eternal nothingness. 

For various reasons, some of which are good, some 
are bad, but none should be held indifferent, this subject 
is one from which most of us are disposed to turn away. 
If the heart is what it ought to be, or even in some fair 
measure is right with God, then sin in its every phase 
and relation is hateful, abominable; and hence even to 
think of it is not a pleasant exercise. And, on the other 
hand, if that triad of evils, the world, and the flesh, and 
the devil, sway the mind, one is loathe to enter into judg- 
ment upon sin; for he knows that he must find much in 


RE a TEI TENTS SALON UOMO ic LOD a 
— 229 — 


s 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


himself which he is unwilling to discover. In such case, 
if sentence is passed at all, it will be of the nature of 
compromise, which is but additional sin. There are yet 
in the world, it must be confessed, those, whom we all 
too much resemble, who 


“Compound for sins they are inclined to 
By damning those they have no mind to.” 


Nothing of all the various phases and aspects of sin 
more signally illustrates its many-sidedness in character 
and its manifoldness in power than does the fact, that 
we most dislike to know the real demerit of the sins which 
we are the least averse to practice. And nothing more 
clearly reveals the cunning craftiness of him by whom 


‘sin came into our world, than does his success in making 


men dishonest with themselves, and willing to have the 
truth hidden from their eyes, or their eyes blinded to the 
truth. The attitude of mind and spirit that alone is fitting 
in our study is that to which one is brought, when, the 
Spirit of God helping his infirmity, he prays: “Open 
thou mine eyes that I may behold wondrous things out 
of thy law.” “Search me, O God, and know my heart; 
try me and know my thoughts; and see if there be any 
wicked way in me, and lead me in the way everlasting.” 

John Calvin’s saying is true: ‘Consciousness of sin 
is the first step to salvation.” 

Of course this paper will not attempt a solution of 
the great mysteries involved in the origin of sin and its 
entrance into our world. The historical character of the 
first chapters of the book of Genesis is recognized as 
positively and definitely as is that of any other portion 
of the book, or, for that matter, of any portion of the 


— 230 — 


co cl Sd US cel Sl ee tha TR eas E 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


tne 
ee a 


entire Bible; and the credibility of the record is just as 
readily acknowledged. While mystery is here, one prin- 
ciple must be steadfastly maintained, namely: that God 
is not—God cannot be—the author of sin; and equally 
absolute is the conviction that “God cannot be tempted. 
with evil, and he himself tempteth no man.” Respecting 
the first mystery that presents itself, namely: “Why was 
the existence of sin tolerated in the creation of a God at 
once eternal, self-existent, and infinite in wisdom, power, 
holiness, and- benevolence?” Dr. A. A. Hodge has said: 
“It is obvious that God has permitted sin, and hence it 
was right for Him to do so. But why it was right must 
ever remain a mystery demanding submission and defying ` 
solution.” 

Respecting a second mystery that confronts us, 
namely: “How could sin originate in the will of a creature 
created with a positively holy disposition?” It may be said: : 
that, in the case of Satan and other angels “Which kept not 
their first estate,” speculation is presumption. The Bible 
has told us nothing, and there is no other possible source of 
information. In the case of Adam and Eve, God permitted 
Satan to tempt them, this fell within His purpose of proba- 
tion, and He permitted them to yield; but He neither 
caused nor approved of the sinful conduct of either party. 
Satan fell untempted—there was no tempter—and so fell 
hopelessly. In this respect Adam’s probation differed from 
that of the angels. The test to which the man was sub- 
jected was the simplest and most gracious and advanta- 
geous that could be proposed. It was not that he must do 
some great thing, but simply to refrain from taking the 
fruit of a single tree. The probation was designed to con- 
firm the holy character that was his by creation, “sufficient ` 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


to have stood though free to fall;” and had it been main- 
tained in spite of Satan’s onslaught, the covenant condition 
of life would have been fulfilled; and sin had never des- 
olated our world—the fair work of God and the goodly 
heritage of man. Beyond these simple representations the 
Bible does not lead us, and the reasoning of understandings 
which never in this life shall wholly be relieved of the dark 
shadow cast by sin, can never reach an indubitable con- 
clusion. 

In this discussion there will be no further reference to 
the philosophy, or to the psychology of sin. Not that these 
disciplines are held in light esteem. That such methods of 
study as these employ have a well established rationale is 
most willingly acknowledged. But the time that is allowed for 
the presentation of this theme, and the object that is sought 
to be attained alike forbid entrance upon such lines of 
study as they mark out. I have chosen to deal alone with 
the Theology of Sin, and the term “Theology” is employed 
with set purpose. Theology is the “Doctrine of God,” and 
if it be that in truth it is God’s Doctrine as well—the teach- 
ing of God’s Word. The phrase, “The Word of God,” and 
the term, “Theology,” are not antagonistic in significance; 
although some false teachers would seem to make them so, 
and some good men, with more zeal than knowledge, would 
seem to think them so. 

In the light of Scripture statement what representa- 
tion may be offered touching the Nature, the Extent, and 
the Effects of Sin? 

I. Nature of Sin. 

As to the first inquiry, it may be noted that a defini- 
tion of Sin was framed by Campegius Vitringa, a repre- 
sentative of the Dutch school of theologians two and one 


— 232 — 


er NEN R SAA 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


quarter centuries ago, which has had all but universal 
recognition by evangelical teachers the world over. In its 
English form it reads: “The nature of sin is disagreement 
of act, habit, or state of a man with the divine law.” Four 
distinguishing features mark this classic definition. It 
recognizes a perfect standard of moral character, in the law 
of God; it supposes rational and voluntary agents as the 
subjects of this law; it requries not only outward com- 
pliance in act, but also inward conformity of heart, and 
constant agreement in life of one who would be held guilt- 
less; and it reckons divergence from this perfect and 
unchangeable standard, whether by excess or defect, by 
omission or by commission, to have the character of sin. 
Will the Bible doctrine of Sin justify the length and 
breadth and depth and height of meaning that are com- 
prehended in the brevity and simplicity of this definition? 
Let us see. Sin according to the Bible view has two 
inalienable characteristics, namely: guilt, or illdesert, lia- 
bility to punishment; and corruption, or defilement. Em- 
phasis is laid upon the first of these in all the sacrifices 
involving the taking of the life of the offering, especially 
in the sin-offerings, whether these had general or individual 
reference. The essential parts of the ritual in such sacri- 
fices were five in number: the presentation of the animal at 
the altar; the imposition of the offerer’s hands; the slaying 
of the victim by him whose sacrifice it was; the disposition 
of the blood by the priest; and the burning of the body 
upon the altar, or “without the camp.” In every such 
sacrifice, through all the centuries of the legal dispensation, 
there was the acknowledgment of guilt; and, in the death 
of the victim, the vicarious submission to the penalty that 
was due to sin in the person of the offerer, In the rivers 


— 233 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


eR eee e eee a E a ee e eea e a o e E E E 


of blood that was thus poured oùt at the altar’s side, from 
the day when the skins of the first substitutes were torn 
from their bleeding bodies to furnish a covering for the 
nakedness of the guilty pair in Eden, unto the day when 
the vail of the temple was rent in twain from the top to 
the bottom in token of the uncovering of the new and 
living way, consecrated by the blood of Jesus into the 
better Paradise, witness was borne to the guilt of human 
sin and the single way of expiation. On the great Day of 
Atonement the ritual provided was peculiarly significant in 
its emphasis of the guilt of sin, and also of the defilement 
that was ever an attendant. It was on this one day of all 
the year that the High Priest might enter into the most 
holy place within the vail and no other man must be 
within the tabernacle on this occasion. First of all the 
High Priest must provide for himself a young bullock for 
a sin-offering, and a ram for a burnt-offering. Then hav- 
ing divested himself of the splendid robes which he was 
wont to wear at times, he washed his flesh with water and 
put on the linen garments specially furnished for this 
service. Next the offering for the people was selected, two 
kids of the goats for a sin-offering, and one ram for a 
burnt-offering. Before the Lord, at the door of the taber- 
nacle the lot was cast upon the two goats determining the 
part each victim must sustain in making atonement for the 
people. Then the bullock of the sin-offering for the High 
Priest and for his house was slain, and, the way of entrance 
into that within the vail having been prepared through the 
cloud of incense smoke from the coals of the censer, the 
blood of this victim was borne within and sprinkled upon 
the mercy-seat eastward and before the mercy-seat seven 
times. Returning to the outer court the High Priest slew
