THE CHURCH, WHICH IS HIS BODY—ITS 
UNITY, ITS CHARACTER, ITS MISSION, | 
ITS DESTINY 


WILLIAM L. PETTINGILL 


Dean of the Philadelphia School of the Bible; pastor of North Bap- 
tist Church at Wilmington, Delaware; editor of “Serving-and- 
Waiting”; author of “Israel, Jehovah’s Covenant 
People,’ “Simple Studies in Matthew,’ “Simple 
Studies in Romans.” “Simple Studies in 
the Revelation,” ete. 


“The church which is His body” is a Biblical expres- 
sion, and it is “the church which is His body” that is to 
occupy our attention in this study. 

There is a church which is the body of Christ, and 
there is a church which is not His body. Indeed, there 
are many of these latter, but there is only one church 
which is His body. It is made up of those, and only 
those, who are born again. 

Churches there are—assemblies, that is, of individuals 
—societies called out and called together for a common 
purpose, and often bearing the name of our Lord and 
Savior Jesus Christ, which nevertheless are not, and are 
not even related to, “the church which is His body, the 
fulness of Him that filleth all in all.” 


I. The Unity of the Church which is His Body. 


“There is one body.” The church of God always has 
been, it now is, and it always shall be one and indivisable. 


Helpful books by the above author listed on pages 446-449, 


— 315 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


In it there is no schism. In it there is unity. It may, and 
it does, take a degree of spiritual and priestly energy to 
sing 

“We are not divided, all one body we; 

One in hope and doctrine, one in charity,” 
but it is the truth, and this truth is finding concrete illus- 
tration and heartening confirmation in this present World 
Conference on Christian Fundamentals. 

Whether men see it or not, there is one body and only 
one. The Scripture insists upon this everywhere, in type 
and symbol as well as by plain precept and teaching. There 
is in the world today a unique thing, unlike any other 
thing, and it is the church of God. I feel insulted every 
time I hear people compare the church with the world’s 
organizations, such as lodges, clubs, societies. There is 
nothing in common between the church of the living God 
and these things. They are organizations, the church is 
an organism. The church of God—in no figurative sense, 
but in a real and vital sense—is the body of Christ. It is 
made up of the “members of His body, of His flesh, and 
of His bones.” Just as truly as that this human body in 
which I live and move, and through which I manifest my- 
self, is my body, so is the church of God the body of the 
Christ—a living, throbbing, breathing vital organism. There 
is nothing else like that in the world. In 1 Cor. 12:12, it is 
written, “For as the body is one, and hath many members, 
and all the members of that one body, being many, are 
yet one body: so also is Christ.” You would expect to 
read there, “so also is the church.” He is plainly talking 
about the church, but He does not name it the church. 
He speaks of the church under the name of Christ, as you 
and I often speak of a man’s body by the man’s name. 


SS SS SA SR SS RS RESON RE NS RTS 


— 316 — 


SS 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


f 
And why not? It is not only true that I live in this thing 
which is my body, but it is also true that this body. is 
myself, and it may therefore be referred to by my name. 
Just so, the church of God is the body of Christ, and 
therefore it properly bears His name. 

The unity of the church which is His body is, as I 
have said, illustrated in such a gathering as this World 
Conference on Christian Fundamentals. Denominations 
are represented here, but there is no denominationalism 
represented here. Episcopalians are here, Baptists are here, 
Presbyterians, Methodists, Reformed Churchmen, Brethren 
(United, Untied, Open and Exclusive) ; but no one is here 
because he is an Episcopalian, or a Baptist, or any of these 
other things. We are here because we are members of the 
one body, having one Spirit, even as we are called in one 
hope of our calling, owning the same Lord, contending for 
the faith once for all delivered unto the saints, baptized 
into Jesus Christ by the one baptism of the Holy Spirit, 
worshiping one God and Father of us all, Who is above all, 
and through all, and in us all. 

The English word unity occurs but three times in the 
whole Bible, and it always refers to the only unity that is 
real, namely, the unity of the Spirit. In the 133d Psalm it 
is written: “Behold, how good and how pleasant it is for 
brethren to dwell together in unity! It is like the precious 
ointment upon the head, that ran down upon the beard, 
even Aaron’s beard: that went down to the skirts of his 
garments; As the dew of Hermon, and as the dew that 
descended upon the mountains of Zion: for there the Lord 
commanded the blessing, even life for ever more.” 

` That is just a beautiful picture of the unity which is 
more than uniformity, more than conformity, more than 


— 317 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


human, and which is brought about, “not by might, nor by 
power, but by my Spirit, saith the Lord.” 

The other two occasions where the word unity is found 
are, both of them, in the 4th chapter of Ephesians. Here 
again the unity in view is the unity of the Holy Spirit— 
the unity which is wrought in the church by the Holy 
Spirit of God. These passages will come before us at a 
later point in this study; they are mentioned here only to 
point out that unity, the only unity with which God is 
concerned, is “the unity of the Spirit,” which we are ex- 
horted to preserve “in the bond of peace.” 


II. The Character of the Church which is His Body. 


Since the church is the body of Christ, it follows of 
necessity that the church has no character but His. Much 
is said in these days about Christian character, and the 
building of it. But, as a plain matter of fact, there is no 
such thing as Christian character, apart from the person 
of Christ. 

We sometimes talk loosely about endeavoring, with 
the Lord’s help, of course, to live a Christian life. There 
is no Christian life but Christ’s. He alone can live the 
Christian life, and, even with His help, which by the way 
has never been promised to us for such a purpose, we can 
never for a single moment live a Christian life. I say He 
has never promised to help us do anything of the kind. 
He lives His own life, manifesting Himself, through His 
people; and wherever He finds a yielded vessel He occupies 
it, living in it, and showing Himself alive in it. 

The Holy Spirit, by whom the church is baptized into 
Jesus Christ, indwells every believer’s body, seeking to 
reveal the Lord Jesus and make Him known. And just in 


— 318 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the measure in which we are yielded to the indwelling, 
Holy Spirit, we bear the fruit of the Spirit—“love, joy, 
peace, longsuffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, meek- 
ness, and self-control,’ —and this is Christian character, the 
character of the church which is the body of Christ, be- 
cause it is the character of Christ. It is His life, mani- 
festing itself in and through His body, just as your life 
manifests itself in and through your body, or my life mani- 
fests itself in and through my body. There is no ‘life, 
apart from Him: “In Him was (and is) life, and the 
life was (and is) the light of men.” He is our life, and 
it is He Himself, living His own life:in us, that produces 
love, joy, peace, and so on. 

Love? There is none, apart from Him. He is God, 
and “God is love.” You know how impossible for you to 
love folks who are unlovely. You have tried it many 
times, and you have always failed. You have said, “I must - 
love everybody. The Bible says I must love even my 
enemies. Now, I do not love my enemies.” You have 
thought of one enemy in particular, and you have said, 
“No; there is no use trying to deceive myself about it, 
I do not love that man. I know I should love him, but 
I do not. But the Bible says I ought to love him. Well, 
then, since I ought to, I must. And if I must, I can. And 
if I can, I will. Now, therefore, go to, I will love that 
man !” 

Do you remember going through something like that 
in your experience? And do you remember what a poor 
sickly thing it was that you brought forth out of all that 
struggle? I ask, “Do you love him now?” And you say, 
“Well, I don’t wish him any harm,” “Yes, yes, but do you 
love him?” Then you say, “Well, it’s all right, but just 


— 319 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


let him keep away from me, and I will keep away from 
him.” And you call that love! It cannot be done; you 
cannot do it, even with the Lord’s help, and He is not help- 
ing you; He will not help you that way. I will tell you 
what to do—just give that job up; stop trying and begin 
trusting. Trust Him. And the first thing you know, there 
will be a warm feeling in your heart for that fellow, and 
you will be praying for him. 

MR. ALEXANDER: Do you love your enemies? 

MR. PETTINGILL: No; the Lord Jesus Christ loves 
my enemies through me. 

And then, there is joy. Joy? there is none, apart from 
Him. It is only when His joy abides in us that our joy is 
full. “In His presence (and there only) is fulness of 
joy; at His right hand there are pleasures for ever more.” 

Peace? ‘There is none, apart from Him. One day the 
world shall know Him as the Prince of Peace, and mean- 
while “He is our peace.” 

Longsuffering? There is none, apart from Him. He 
it is Who is pictured in the thirteenth chapter of First 
Corinthians, Who “suffereth long, and is kind.” 

Gentleness? There is none, apart from Him. It was 
a man of war who wrote of Him, “Thy gentleness hath 
made me great.” 

Goodness? There is none, apart from Him. Accord- 
ing to His own testimony, “there is none good but one, 
that is God.” In us, that is in our flesh, dwelleth no good 
thing; but in Him dwelleth all the fulness of the God- 
head bodily, and we are complete in Him; He is our 
goodness. 

Faith? There is none, apart from Him. “He abideth 
faithful.” 


SS 


— 320 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


-AL 
ed 


Meekness? There is none, apart from Him; He alone 
is “meek, and lowly in heart.” 

Self-control? There is none, apart from Him. He 
never spoke rashly with His tongue, He never had a re- 
bellious or fretful thought. Be careful for nothing, since 
He is in charge. 

This is His character, and hence His body’s char- 
acter; this is His life, and therefore ours. This is He, 
Himself—God—Who worketh in us, both to will His good 
pleasure and to do it. 

III. The Mission of the Church which is His Body. 

This may be put in a word: the sole mission of the 
church is its own completion. The Spirit of Truth goes 
into this matter very fully and clearly in the 4th chapter 
of the Epistle to the Ephesians. I desire to go with you 
to that chapter; and here we return to the thought of the 
body’s unity, in connection with our inquiry concerning 
the body’s mission. I read: “I therefore, the prisoner of 
the Lord, beseech you that ye walk worthy of the vocation 
wherewith ye are called, with all lowliness and meekness, 
with longsuffering, forbearing one another in love; en- 
deavoring to keep the unity of the Spirit in the bond of 
peace” ‚(verses 1-3). 

“Endeavoring” is not the right word here; “giving all 
diligence” is better: “giving all diligence to preserve the 
unity of the Spirit in the bond of peace.” 

Then follows a description of this unity, the unity of 
the Spirit, to preserve which we are “to give all diligence ;” 
it is sevenfold; that is, complete: “There is one body, and 
one Spirit, even as ye are called in one hope of your calling; 
one Lord, one faith, one baptism, one God and Father of 


— 321 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


all, who is above all, and through all, and in you all” 
(verses 4-6). 

Now, in order that the church which is His body may 
be completed, the risen Christ has bestowed upon it certain 
gifts. In that other gift chapter, the 12th of First Corin- 
thians, the gifts are bestowed upon men by the Spirit of 
God—“the manifestation of the Spirit is given to every 
man to profit withal.” Here in Ephesians the gifts are 
these men themselves, already gifted by the Holy Spirit. 
The risen Christ gives these gifted men to His church, for 
its complete upbuilding: “And he gave some, apostles; 
and some, prophets; and some, evangelists; and some, 
pastors and teachers” (verse 11). 

The apostles are gone, and they have no “successors.” 
They need none, for we have their complete testimony in 
the New Testament Scriptures, constituting a sufficient and 
authoritative rule of faith and practice. The other “gifts” 
are still with us, raised up as needed by the risen Christ 
Himself as Head over all things to the church which is 
His body. The New Testament prophet is one who “speak- 
eth unto men to edification, and exhortation, and com- 
fort” (1 Cor. 14:3). The evangelist is he that preaches 
the evangel, the good news, the gospel of our salvation. 
The pastor is the under-shepherd, appointed by the: Chief 
Shepherd to feed and tend the sheep and lambs of His 
flock during His own absence from them. And the teacher 
is the one appointed from on high to expound and unfold 
the treasures of the Word of God, in order that the chil- 
dren might be fed with the Bread of God. 

Some one says: “Certainly! we understand about that. 
These are ‘the clergy” They are appointed to look after 
‘the laity?” That is all quite right and perfectly clear. 


— 322 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


“The clergy—that is, the reverends—are to ‘conduct divine 
service’, the clergy are to ‘preach the Word’, the clergy are 
to ‘administer the sacraments’, the clergy are to ‘pronounce 
the benediction’; and the laity—well, the laity may pay the 
bills.” 

What a mischievous notion this is! Why cannot we 
rid ourselves, once and for ever, of these rags of Roman- 
ism? Why must we go on with these relics of Paganism, 
these inventions of Satan, tending to rob us of our common 
wealth in the Lord, our common brotherhood in Christ, our 
blessed fellowship in the Holy Spirit, and leading us to 
think of “the ministry” as something confined to the man 
in the pulpit, something apart from the man in the pew, 
something even denied to him, because he is “not or- 
dained”? God help us! What confusion we have come to! 
What a condition of things! Let us get back to our Book. 
Let us see “what saith the Lord.” 

I read on: “For the perfecting of the saints, for the 
work of the ministry, for the edifying of the body of 
Christ” (verse 12). 

We must read this verse with care. It is a statement 
of the purpose of Christ in bestowing His gifts, that is 
these gifted men, upon His church. We must not read 
it as a threefold statement. It is not (1) for the perfecting 
of the saints: then (2) for the work of the ministry; and, 
finally (3) for the edifying of the body of Christ. No; 
it is a single statement. Christ’s purpose in the bestowal 
of His gifts is, in a word, the edifying of His body, the 
church; and in order to this end He must have ministry; 
and in order that there may be ministry the saints must 
be perfected, or trained; and in order that the saints may 
be perfected or trained, certain men have been gifted by 


— 323 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the Holy Spirit and then they themselves have been given 
by the risen Christ to His church. The Revision is a great 
improvement: “For the perfecting of the saints unto the 
work of ministering;” and so is the 1911 Bible: “For the 
perfecting of the saints unto the doing of service.” And 
always with this end in view: “the edifying (that is, the 
building up, the upbuilding, the completion) of the body 
of Christ,” which is the church. 

Thus, as we have already said, the mission of the 
church is its own completion. This becomes even more 
clear as we go on with this passage: “Till we all come in 
the unity of the faith, and of the knowledge of the Son 
of God, unto a perfect man, unto the measure of the stature 
of the fulness of Christ” (verse 13). 

This present ministry-work is to go on until the body 
is completed. Then we shall all have come “unto a perfect 
man.” 

Not “unto perfect manhood.” That is not at all the 
meaning of the phrase. It is this: God is now at work, 
making a Man. How long it took to make the first man, 
we may not know; but for nearly two thousand years He 
has been at work making the last Man. That Man is 
Christ. His body is the church. Whenever a sinner is 
converted to Him, a new member is added to His body, 
and the body is that much nearer completion. This work 
is to go on until the last sinner to be converted in this 
dispensation is turned unto God, and then the perfect Man 
will be complete, having arrived at “the measure of the 
stature of the fulness of Christ.” 

The Man-child is not yet fully born; the moment He 
is fully born He will be “caught up unto God and to His 
throne” (Rev. 12:5).

| 
| 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


The mystery of godliness includes the church which 
is Christ’s body. 

When this is seen, the sequence of 1 Timothy 3:16 
becomes clear: 

“God was manifest in the flesh” That is the incarna- 
tion, the Son of God in the Seed of the woman. 

“Justified in the Spirit” That is His resurrection 
from the dead, by which He was “declared to be the Son 
of God with power, according to the Spirit of Holiness” 
(Rom. 1:4). 

“Seen of angels.” That is His present session at the 
right hand of the Majesty on high, our Advocate, our 
Intercessor, the great High Priest over the house of God. 

“Preached unto the Gentiles.” That is our present 
work, appointed of Him who is our Head, and it is by 
this means that the body is to be completed. 

“Believed on in the world.” So He is; thank God! 
We believe on Him; thousands have believed and are 
believing on Him; myriads, millions, have believed and go 
on believing. So shall it be, until the Man-child is born. 
And then,— 

“Received up into glory.” When? The moment the 
Man-child is born. 

“And when shall that be? The Lord, Who knoweth 
them that are His, has not been pleased to inform us of 
the times or the seasons. I know not when. 


“T only know that He is near 
And that His voice I soon shall hear.” 
_The church is a people gathered out from among the 
nations for His name. The church is to build up itself, it 
is to come to completion, by obeying His command to carry 


— 325 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the gospel to the nations. “Ye shall be witnesses unto 
Me, * * * unto the uttermost part of the earth.” 
This is our task, and to this task we are appointed. This 
is the goal, to be ever before our vision; in order that we 
“may grow up into Him in all things, which is the Head, 
even Christ: from whom the whole body fitly joined to- 
gether and compacted by that which every joint supplieth, 
according to the effectual working in the measure of every 
part, maketh increase of the body unto the edifying (that 
is, the building up, the upbuilding, the completion) of itself 
in love” (Eph. 4:15, 16). 


IV. The Destiny of the Church which is His Body. 


This, too, may be stated in a single phrase. The 
destiny of the church is to “be for ever with the Lord.” 
And what mortal man can begin to tell the meaning of 
that? Truly, it “passeth all understanding.” Wondrous 
things await the church of God, things which it is not 
possible for a man to utter. We do not know, we may 
not know—not now, for now we could not bear it—all 
that awaits us. But enough is known, enough is revealed, 
to wean us from the things of earth and time, and fill our 
souls with rapture on account of the entrancing vision. 
For He hath “made known unto us the mystery of His 
will, according to His good pleasure which he hath pur- 
posed in Himself: that in the dispensation of the fulness 
of times He might gather together in one all things in 
Christ, both which are in heaven, and which are on earth; 
even in Him, * * * that we should be to the praise of 
His glory * * * and * * * that in the ages to come He 
might show the exceeding riches of His grace in His kind- 
ness toward us through Christ Jesus” (Eph, 1:9-12; 2:7). 


— 326 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


We have read His will, His “last will and testament”: 
“Father, I will that they also, whom thou hast given me, 
be with me where I am; that they may behold my glory, 
which thou hast given me; for thou lovedst me before 
the foundation of the world. O righteous Father, the 
world hath not known Thee: but I have known Thee, and 
these have known that Thou hast sent me. And I have 
declared unto them Thy name, and will declare it: that the 
love wherewith thou hast loved me may be in them, and I 
in them” (John 17: 24-26). 

That is enough—quite enough! “The Lord Himself 
shall descend from heaven * * * and we shall be caught 
up * * * to meet the Lord in the air. And so shall we 
ever be with the Lord. Wherefore comfort one another 
with these words.” 

“For ever with the Lord!’ 
Amen! so let it be! 

Life from the dead is in that word, 
’Tis immortality. 

Here in the body pent, 
Absent from Him we roam, 


Yet nightly pitch our moving tent 
A day’s march nearer home. 


“For ever with the Lord!’ 
Father if ’tis thy will, 

The promise of that faithful word 
E’en here to us fulfil. 

Knowing as we are known, 
How shall we love that word, 

And oft repeat before the throne, 
‘For ever with the Lord!”

WHY I PREACH THE SECOND COMING | 


I. M. HALDEMAN 


Pastor First Baptist Church, New York City. 


A Synopsis of the Address 


Reasons for preaching the Second Coming of our 
Lord Jesus Christ are manifold. The statement of a few 
will be sufficient. ' 

I. It is the one event most often recorded in holy 
Scripture. 

The Holy Spirit sets it forth in type, in symbol, in 
plain declaration and prophetic forecast. 

The first promise of it is made in Eden in the assur- 
ance given to the woman that her seed should bruise the 
serpent’s head. That act of bruising takes place at the 
Second Coming of our Lord Jesus Christ as recorded. in 
the twentieth chapter of the Revelation, where He is seen 
to descend from heaven and bind Satan under His feet and 
the feet of His saints for a thousand years. As this bind- 
ing, and therefore bruising, occurs at the Second Coming 
and the announcement of it is made in Eden, then in 
Eden you have the first announcement and promise of the 
Second Coming. 

The Lord caught Enoch up to meet Him and took 
him to heaven without dying. Enoch is a figure of the 
generation that will be alive at the Second Coming of the 
Lord who will be caught up to meet Him; and by so much 
is a prophecy of that event. 


Helpful books by the above author listed on pages 446-449. 


ie Kc 0 pee 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Noah preached righteousness, builded an ark for the 
saving of his family. Without warning the Lord called him 
into the ark. After seven days the flood fell and destroyed 
an ungodly world. When the waters subsided Noah and 
his family came forth to set up the new administration of 
God on the earth. Our Lord says His Second Coming 
will be as it was in the days of Noah. Like him, the 
church will be snatched suddenly out of a world under 
doom and taken to the place of safety in heaven. At the 
end of at least seven years the Lord will come forth with 
His church to execute judgment and set up the kingdom 
of righteousness. 

The Lord compares His Second Coming to the days 
of Lot. As it was in the days of Lot so shall it be when 
the Son of Man cometh. Lot dwelt in Sodom and was 
occupied with its interests, vexing his righteous but un- 
spiritual soul with its wickedness. Suddenly the Lord 
snatched him out of the doomed city and the next morning 
destroyed it by fire. Like Lot, Christians who are righteous 
but not spiritual, who are seeking to reform a world under 
doom, will be snatched out of it to meet a descending Lord 
and with Him take part in the execution of judgment upon 
all mere flesh be it good or bad. 

Abraham sends his servant to get a bride for his absent 
son. He finds her, shows her the gifts sent from the 
father in the name of the son, leads her forth to meet him. 
He comes suddenly and takes her to the place prepared. 
The Holy Spirit as the executive of Godhead has come 
from the Father in the name of the Son to secure Him a 
bride. The bride is the church. The Holy Spirit has be- 
stowed gifts upon the church, gifts sent from the Father 
in the name of the Son. He is seeking to lead her out 


— 330 — 


ee ee ee 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


to meet Him. The admonition is: “Behold, the bride- 
groom cometh; go ye out to meet him.” Any moment He 
may come and take her into the place prepared. 


Jacob fleeing from home, lights on a certain place 
at night, takes a stone for his pillow and lies down to 
sleep. In his sleep he has the vision of a ladder reaching 
to heaven with the Lord and His hosts at the top. The 
Lord enters into a covenant with him that the land upon 
which he is lying shall be his and his posterity after him 
forever. As this covenant can be fulfilled only when the 
Lord Himself as the God of Jacob shall come, the vision 
is a symbolic prophecy of that Coming. 

Joseph is rejected by his brethren, sent into a far 
country, even Egypt, is exalted to the throne, becomes a 
savior of the people in the time of famine, gets a Gentile 
bride, is made known to his brethren the second time, 
delivers them from tribulation and brings them to dwell in 
the promised land of Goshen. Rejected by His brethren the 
Jews, exiled into the far country of heaven, exalted to the 
Father’s throne, become the Savior of a famine smitten 
world, our Lord Jesus Christ will make Himself known to 
the Jews a second time. He will ‘appear from heaven in 
glory, deliver them from the great tribulation and bring 
them with all Israel into the promised land of Palestine. 

Moses crosses the Red Sea dry shod and on the deliver- 
ance side sings of the Second Coming of the Lord as a 
man of war. 

David strikes his harp and in the quivering notes of 
his psalms pours forth his testimony to the wonder and 
glory of the Second Coming. 

~ In the Song of Solomon the bridegroom like the Lord 
descends. The bride like the church goes up to meet Him 


— 331 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


and, leaning on His arm, ascends into the pavillion of His 
love. 

Isaiah sees the Lord coming with chariots of glory. 

Jeremiah announces the Lord is coming to make Jeru- 
salem His throne and gather all Israel unto Him. 

Ezekiel beholds Him coming from the way of the east 
and so literally dwelling in Jerusalem that henceforth the 
name of the city will be “The Lord is there.” 
_ Daniel has a vision of Him coming in the clouds of 
heaven. 

Zechariah says when the Lord comes a second time 
His feet in that day shall stand upon the Mount of Olives. 

Malachi compares the Second Coming to the rising of 
the sun. 

From the Old Testament we pass to the New. 

In the gospel according to Matthew the Lord speaks 
continually of His Second Coming. 

In Mark He bids us watch for it. 


In Luke, like the nobleman, He has gone away into a 
far country to receive the title deeds of His kingdom and 
return. 

In John He goes to heaven to prepare a place for the 
church and when it is prepared will come again to receive 
her to Himself. 

In the book of Acts the angels tell the sorrowing 
disciples that this same Jesus Who is taken up into heaven 
shall so come again in like manner as they have seen 
Him go into heaven. 

Romans gives us the promise of the bruising and over- 
throw of Satan at the Second Coming. 


— 332 — 


ee 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


In First Corinthians the Lord comes to raise the dead 
who have fallen asleep in Him. In the Second He is 
coming to transfigure the living who are His. 


Philippians bids us look for a Savior from heaven who 
shall change our bodies and make them like His own. 


Colossians assures us our life is hid with Christ in 
God and when He shall appear we shall appear in glory 
with Him. 

Both Epistles to the Thessalonians are devoted to the 
Second Coming. Every chapter closes with a testimony 
tout 


In First Timothy the Lord comes as King. In Second 
as Judge. 

In Hebrews the coming of the Lord is distinctively 
spoken of as a “Second” Coming. 

James insists “the coming of the Lord draweth nigh.” 

In First Peter the Lord comes to justify the faith of 
His elect. In Second He comes to bring in the new heaven 
and the new earth. 

In First John the Lord is coming to make manifest the 
divine sonship of all believers. In Second He warns that 
all those who deny the Lord is coming again in the flesh 
are antichrists. ; 

Jude gives a description of the coming of the Lord 
with ten thousands of His saints and says this is the testi- 
mony of Enoch, the seventh from Adam. 


The book of the Revelation closes the Bible and is 
given up entirely to the Second Coming. The Second 
Coming is the one theme of the book. It ends with the 
assurance of the Lord that He will come quickly and 


— 333 — 


D 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


en a E 


records the last prayer of the apostolic church: “Amen. 
Even so, come, Lord Jesus.” 

In the New Testament alone there are more than 
three hundred references to the Second Coming. 

If frequency of statement be proof of importance, 
then the importance of the Second Coming considered as 
an event and doctrine cannot be over-estimated. 


II. The Second Coming of our Lord Jesus Christ is 
bound up with every fundamental doctrine, every sublime 
promise and every exhortation to high and practical Chris- 
tian living. 

It is bound up with the doctrine of the resurrection, 
with the promise of rewards, with the victory of God over 
Satan. Because of the imminence of the Second Coming, 
we are exhorted to live blamelessly before men and holily 
before God. As preachers we are to feed the flock and 
preach the Word in season and out of season. 

The man who teaches the Second Coming is an im- 
practicable doctrine does not know the Bible. He has failed 
to hear the assurance of the Holy Ghost-inspired Apostle 
John that every one who has his hope on the coming of 
the Lord purifies his heart even as He is pure. 

III. At the Second Coming of our Lord Jesus Christ 
alone will redemption be complete and the blood of the 
cross be justified. 

The Son of God did not come into the world and go 
through the agony of the cross just to make men moral. 
He died that He might deliver men from death. Death is 
the blackest blot on the earth. It is the work of him who 
had the power of death—that is—the Devil. The Lord 
died to destroy the power and the work of the Devil. He 
died to make men immortal. No one on earth has immor- 


A 


a TED 


GOD HATH SPOKEN. 


tality. There is no such thing as an immortal soul. By 
that it is not meant the soul can cease to exist. It cannot. 
It will exist forever whether saved or lost. “Immortal,” 
“Immortality,” are words never applied in Scripture to the 
soul, but always to the body. To be immortal is to have a 
deathless, incorruptible body like that of the Lord. He 
did not come to make us eternal, even if happy ghosts. 
That would be a scandal to His own immortality. He 
came to die, pay the penalty of death on behalf of all who 
should believe on Him, rise from the dead and as the 
Second Adam, the life giver, give deathless bodies to the 
souls of His redeemed. He died for their bodies as well 
as souls. He will give this immortality to the Christian 
dead by resurrection and to His living saints by transfigura- 
tion. This resurrection and transfiguration will take place 
at His Second Coming. Not till the Lord comes the second 
time will Christians become immortal, redemption be made 
complete and the blood of the cross justified. 

IV. The church cannot enter in and fulfill her true 
function as co-ordinate ruler with the Lord over the world 
till He comes the second time. 

The church was not sent out to convert the world, but 
to preach the gospel to every creature. 

The church is not here to condone the world but to 
condemn it. 

The world is as a ship pounding to pieces on the rocks. 
The church is not to paint and patch it. Her work is like 
that of men in the life boat, get as many as possible out 
of the fated ship and bring them to shore. Get people out 
of Adam into Christ. 

~The church is not a kingdom. The real church is a 
bride waiting for the bridegroom. That was her original 


— 335 — 


ne 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


p 
I2 


attitude. She was to serve the Lord while waiting for 
His return. She was to have the upward look. She 
changed the heaven uplook for the world outlook, and fell 
under the temptation her Lord had overcome. He was 
offered the kingdoms of the world and refused them. The 
church accepted the Devil’s offer, took the world as her 
paramour and, as a result of that union, brought forth the 
Roman Catholic Church. The Reformation came. Protes- 
tantism rejected the temporalities of Rome but held on to 
the idea of a kingdom. Protestantism has paid the penalty 
of that concept. It has become of the earth earthy. Seek- 
ing to establish a spiritual kingdom on earth, it has lost 
the vision of heaven and ceased to be spiritual. The whole 
aim of modern and advanced Protestantism is to make the 
world better and more comfortable for the natural man 
to live in, and to make the natural man morally and civically 
better to live in it. Its leading preachers and claimed 
thinkers have turned their backs on the fundamental doc- 
trines of Christianity, have rejected the Bible as the fully 
inspired and inerrant Word of God, have thrown hereafter 
things overboard, and given themselves up to living and 
working for the present world and the life that now is. 
In short, Protestantism has become a society for ethical 
culture, an organization for competitive morality, and like 
salt that has lost its savor will sooner or later be repudiated 
by the Son of God and trampled under foot of men. 


The church cannot reign while the Lord is still a re- 
jected king: Only when He comes can she take her place 
as queen and enter upon associated rule with Him. Not 
till He comes will the kingdoms of this world become the 
kingdoms of our Lord and His Christ—the Anointed—the 
Church. 


— 336 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


V. Only at the Second Coming of our Lord Jesus 
Christ will the covenant promises to Israel be fulfilled. 


God promised the land of Palestine to Abraham and 
his posterity for an everlasting possession. He never got 
a foot of it under the covenant. 


Israel never got it under the covenant of grace, but 
under the covenant of the law. They failed to keep the 
law after they got in and were at last cast out. Ten tribes 
were carried away and have been nationally hidden for 
twenty-five hundred years. For two thousand years the 
two remaining tribes, the Jews, have been at home in every 
land but their own. 


But God cannot lie. They are to have the land. There- 
fore they must return to it. The Book is crowded with 
the promises of return. Many are there now, more than 
any day since the captivity of Babylon. The promises are 
in a measure being fulfilled. It is said active prepara- 
tions are being made for the going back of over two mil- 
lions, a large proportion of them from Russia. They will 
go back in unbelief, will accept a false Messiah and suffer 
unspeakable anguish under him. At the heighth of their 
suffering the Lord will come with His saints from Mount 
Sinai whither He will first descend, overthrow the Anti- 
christ, deliver the elect remnant, regenerate them and 
make them the spiritual and political head of the nations. 
The very air is full of the sounds of their returning. 
Jerusalem has already been restored to them. The flag 
of David has been unfurled. The Lord must come to keep 
faith with their fathers and to fulfill His own word after 
He had officially set them aside for this age; when He 
said: “Behold, your house is left unto you desolate. For 


— 337 — . 


a 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


I say unto you, Ye shall not see me henceforth, till ye say, 
Blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord.” 

VI. Only when our Lord comes will a government of 
everlasting righteousness be established on the earth. 

God would have made Israel the head of the nations, 
sent His incarnate Son and through Him and them ruled 
the world in righteousness and peace. Israel faiied to 
meet the divine opportunity. The proposed rulership of 
the world was taken away from the Jews as the remaining 
representatives of Israel, and given to the Gentiles. God 
would try the Gentiles. He would give to them the privi- 
lege of administering government in righteousness. This 
special opportunity is called the “Times of the Gentiles.” 
This era began officially under Nebuchadnezzar, when the 
living God definitely through Daniel the prophet, who 
interpreted the king’s dream, assured him that he was the 
official head and beginning of Gentile world rulership. 

Speaking in the name of the Lord, Daniel said: “Thou, 
O king, art a king of kings: for the God of heaven hath 
given thee a kingdom, power, and strength and glory. And 
wheresoever the children of men dwell, the beasts of the 
field, and the fowls of the heaven, hath he given into thine 
hand, and hath made thee ruler over them all.” 

This rulership, ordained of God, came on down in 
strict prophetic and providential succession through Medo- 
Persia, Greece and Rome to the descendants of those 
peoples and kingdoms of our day. For twenty-five hun- 
dred years the Gentile nations, dwelling in the territory 
once occupied by Rome in its greatest extent, have ruled 
the world, dictating its policies and affecting all peoples. 
From this prophetic and topographical center have gone 
forth all the shaping and moulding of civilizations. This 


a

has been the center of determining government on earth, 
this the controlling heart of the world; and while the 
Gentiles so ruled, the city that God would have made the 
Capital of the earth, Jerusalem, has been trodden down 
and over run by Gentile feet, exactly as the Son of God 
foretold. 

For twenty-five hundred years the Gentiles in their 
times have administered government and to this very hour 
that administration of government has been a colossal, a 
costly, a bloody and a ghastly failure. Millions of lives 
have been sacrificed, streams of treasure have been poured 
forth, lands have been desolated, civilizations have been 
destroyed, agony has walked broadcast with its twin sisters 
of famine and pestilence, death has been victor and king; 
and actual blood enough it is said has been shed to float 
all the ships of the world—an ocean of blood and a cease- 
less rain of tears—twenty-five hundred years of tyranny, 
despotism, revolution, right upon the scaffold, wrong upon 
the throne, wealth for the few, misery for the many and 
speechless sorrow and bitter discontent brooding over all. 

In all these years man has been unable to govern 
himself ; and now we are in the aftermath of the greatest, 
the most cruel, most selfish, most excuseless and most 
savage war the world has ever known. The days are full 
of peril; men’s hearts are failing them for fear; perplexity 
is on every side; the wisdom of statesmen is as folly, and 
while they deliberate, legislate and dream of leagues of 
peace, anarchy, bolshevism and devil-inspired lawlessness 
lift their hydra-heads. 

_ The world needs the hand of imperial power to be 
laid upon its fever pulses; that imperial power is in the 
hand of Him who is not yet, but Who is to be when He 


ee aaa 


— 339 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


comes, the Prince of Peace. The world needs Him, its 
growing disasters are crying out for Him. And He is 
coming. He is coming as the Prince of Peace to make 
peace and keep it. He is not coming with the olive branch 
and the dove. He is not coming with soft-spoken words 
and extended hands of benediction. He is coming with 
a rod of iron, with eyes like a flame of fire and a voice 
as the sound of many waters when they thunder on the 
trembling shore. He is coming to break to pieces and 
overthrow all authority but His own. He is coming to 
destroy all iniquity and to set up the kingdom of righteous- 
ness and truth. It will be a kingdom in which peace shall 
flow as a river and life shall be worth while. 

Not till He comes will nations beat their swords into 
plowshares and their spears into pruning hooks. 

VII. Only when the Lord comes will the earth be 
delivered from the bondage of corruption. 

Man was set up as the head of the earth. When he 
fell the earth was carried down with him. It was put 
under a ban and a curse. Its fruitfulness was restrained. 
Briars and thorns and weeds were given unlimited liberty 
to grow, all grounds and every soil should give them wel- 
come. No culture is required for thorns, nor weeds, nor 
creeping, poisonous things. He who would have fruitful 
orchard and ripening harvests must bend his back and toil. 
The earth is full of corruption, dead men’s bones and 
putrefying things that once had life. The whole earth 
feels the ban and is sick of corruption’s seal. 

We are told the whole creation groaneth and travail- 
eth together in pain until now; that like one on the tiptoe 
of expectation who stretches out his neck and thrusts for- 
ward his head that he may see, and waits for the vision, 


POY ee 


° 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


so creation is thrilling with pain, with hope, with fear and 
in expectation is waiting for the coming of the Son of 
God and the many sons of glory; waiting and expecting 
that at this coming and apocalypse of splendor and power 
it shall be delivered into the liberty of these very sons of 
glory. Only when He comes Who is the new and perfect 
head of a new and perfect creation will the curse be re- 
moved, the ban taken off and the earth be free. Then, 
indeed, shall the desert be made to blossom as the rose, the 
wilderness become a fruitful garden and all the land as 
Eden. 


VIII. The second coming of our Lord Jesus Christ 
for His church is the most imminent event. on the horizon 
of time. 

Between us and the appearing of our Lord Jesus 
Christ to set up His kingdom at Jesuralem there are many 
foretold and sequential events. Between us and the secret, 
sudden coming of the Lord into the air to take His church 
up to Himself and thence into the place prepared in heaven 
there is not a single predicted event. That coming might 
take place at any moment. 

The Lord Himself has said so. He said: “Watch 
therefore; for ye know not what hour your Lord doth 
come.” 


If we do not know what hour, we do not know what 
moment; and, so far as we are concerned, so far as our 
knowledge goes, it might be any hour and therefore, 
necessarily, any moment. 

The whole value of the exhortation and commanded 
attifude depend upon the sincerity and integrity of the 
Son of God, 


a 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


If He meant what He said, then we are to watch for 
His coming. To watch means to be ready—to expect. 

If He Whom we cal! Lord be not a deceiver, then I 
am under bonds to obey His command (for it is a com- 
mand as absolute as the law of Sinai)—I am to be ready. 
I am actually to expect His coming any day and, therefore, 
every day. 

If Jesus Christ be the truth teller, very truth itself 
and not the concrete of falsehood, if He be not the great 
lie instead of God the truth, if He be as He is—God 
Himself incarnate, His coming for the church is the most 
imminent event for any possible morning or noon or night 
of any day. 

What would it mean if He should suddenly come into 
the upper air? The answer comes quickly and plainly 
enough. It would mean the resurrection of our beloved 
dead who confessed faith upon His name; it would mean 
the transfiguration into His likeness, the clothing upon 
with His immortality of all the living who believe. 


There are preachers who tell us the sudden coming of . 


the Lord would derange the affairs of the world and put 
an end to all human progress. The proposition is pitiable 
and the objection valueless. If the Lord should come His 
coming would put an end to the progress of sin and un- 
righteousness. It would lift man as man into the perfec- 
tion of bodily life and existence. It would give him 
holiness of character and righteousness in dealing man 
with man. It would put an end to selfishness, ambition, 
jealousy, envy and unprincipled rivalry. It would make 
war and the disasters of war impossible. It would banish 
poverty and take away the root of evil in money. It would 
unlock the gates of knowledge, solve the mysteries of

nature and in a moment achieve for man what all his 
education and science have failed to do. It would make 
him co-heir with God, filled with divine power and a 
co-worker with the Lord in turning paradise lost into 
paradise regained. 

It is not by the curricule of the college, nor the enact- 
ments of the halls of legislation, that the world is to be 
swung back to God and to take part once more in the 
music of the spheres; not by evolution is man to climb to 
his originally ordained place of dominion as the god of 
this earth—but by the coming of the Son of Man as his 
very God and Lord. 

Do you want him to come? 

Whom we love we wish, when absent from us, to 
return. 

Today the Lord is saying to the church as of old He 
said to Simon Peter: ‘“Lovest thou me more than these?” 
“Lovest thou Me more than the world and the things that 
are in the world; more than the work you are seeking to 
do for Me; more than any ambition or glory that may come 
to you while I am away?” 

Do you individually love Him above all things else? 

Would you like to see Him—look into His face, touch 
His hands wounded for you, have Him look into your face 
and call you softly and lovingly by your name? 

If you love Him personally, you will want Him to 
come and come personally. 

The last recorded prayer of the apostolic church is the 
record of its heart-beat. 

This is the cry of the church when it stood nearest to 
the Lord. Listen to it: “Amen. Even so, come, Lord

He is listening. 

He wants to hear the church, His true regenerated 
church, lift up that prayer and say it with one living,

so, come, Lord Jesus.”

PROPHECY—WHY STUDY IT? 
WILLIAM L. PETTINGILL 


Much is said to discourage the study of prophecy. 
We are warned that it is speculative; that nobody can get 
anywhere, anyhow, in the matter of assured results. In- 
deed, one would suppose from what one hears that the’ 
only way in the world to get assured results is to join the 
higher critics, for they are the ones who have a patent 
right on that phrase, “assured results.” They are fond of 
referring to “the assured results of modern research.” So 
we are told we ought not to try to study prophecy because 
it is impossible of understanding. It doesn’t get you any- 
where, and then again, it is dangerous, for so many people 
“go crazy” along the line of prophecy. They say it makes 
“faddists,” and “cranks,” and “general all-round nui- 
sances’’—this study of prophecy. 

And we must admit that there is some apparent ground 
for this criticism, for there is scarcely one of the modern 
“isms” and errors that have grown popular which has failed 
to attach itself to the prophetic Word in some way. Almost 
every one of them has something to say about eschatology, 
the doctrine of future things; and as the church generally 
is dumb on that whole subject, it gives room for’ these false 
prophets to gain a foothold among the people of the church 
who are hungry to know what the future has in store. Let 
a Seventh Day Adventist come along and set up a tent in 
the woods outside the town—no matter how far outside the 
town it may be—with a message, any message, concerning 


ae we 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the advent of the Lord Jesus Christ, His second coming, 
and he will get a flock of people to hear him and to believe 
him—to hear him, because they are hungry to hear some- 
thing about the second coming of Christ; and to believe 
him, because so many of God’s people are not taught in 
these things and they do not know the truth from false- 
hood. So, as I say, all this makes these arguments against 
the study of prophecy seem plausible. 

And yet they all break down upon examination of the 
Word of God. There is ample reason in the Word of God, 
in what God Himself has said with reference to the subject 
of prophecy, abundant reason why His children should all 
of them engage in serious study of what God has said with 
reference to future things. 

I have seven reasons to advance for the study of 
prophecy. 

1. We should study prophecy in order that we may 
know God’s mind. In Amos 3:7 it is written, “Surely the 
Lord God will do nothing, but he revealeth his secret unto 
his servants the prophets.” I want to know what God is 
doing. I want to know what God proposes to do. I want 
to know what is the next thing in God’s program, if it has 
pleased Him to reveal it. I know that the things that are 
not revealed belong to God—the secret things. But the 
things that are revealed belong to us and our children, that 
we may know and do His will. How can we know His 
will except we know what His purpose is? And how can 
we know His will is being done unless we know what that 
will and purpose and program may be? Since God has 
given me a hint like this as to how to learn His secret, I 
want to take advantage of it. It is as if He had said to 
me, “My child, if you want to know my secrets, I have re- 


2346 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


vealed them to my servants the prophets, and they can tell 
them to you.” So I go to the prophets to learn the secrets 
of the Lord, which are unto them that fear Him. That is 
the first reason, and if there were no other reasons, I pro- 
test that is reason enough for the study of the prophetic 
Word—that we may know God’s mind. 


2. The second reason for studying phophecy is, for 
our own blessing. There is no subject concerning which 
there is such promise of blessing in the Scriptures as the 
study of the prophetic Word. In Revelation 1:3 it is writ- 
ten, “Blessed is he that readeth, and they that hear the 
words of this prophecy, and keep those things which are 
written therein.” Have you ever heard how Dr. James H. 
Brookes became a premillennialist? Doctor Brookes was 
pastor of the Washington Avenue Presbyterian Church in 
St. Louis, Mo., whose present pastor is in this Conference 
and was yesterday on this platform—Dr. W. Irving Car- 
roll. Doctor Brookes tells how he became a premillen- 
nialist. He said that when one day in his family worship 
he came to the end of the Epistle of Jude the next morning 
he turned back to the first chapter of Matthew and began 
again the reading of the New Testament with his family. 


Upon reaching his study afterwards he heard some- 
thing inside of him say, “Why did you do that thing? And 
why do you always do that thing? You are a preacher of 
the Word of God; you believe that the book of The Rev- 
elation is included in the Word of God; you would not 
think of buying a Bible which did not contain that book. 
You would say that a Bible that did not contain that book 
was‘mutilated. And yet you never do read that book, pri- 
vately or publicly; you never think of preaching on any- 


oe ye 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


thing in that book. Now, why do you do that? Why do 
you always do that? Why are you doing that now?” 

Doctor Brookes says: “I tried to answer; I said: 
‘The book of The Revelation is too difficult; I am unable 
to understand it.’ 

“And the answer was ready. The answer was: ‘You 
never have given the book any serious attention; you have 
never really tried to understand it. Why is it you give up 
so easily on that book? You do not give up other things 
that way. You go through with them.’”’ 

“Well,” says the Doctor, “I had no answer for that.” 
So he turned to the book in his private study and began to 
read. He says: “I learned in the very first line that what 
I always supposed about the book wasn’t true at all. I had 
supposed the book was ‘The Revelation of St. John, the 
Divine,’ but I learned from the first line, that it is ‘The 
Revelation of Jesus Christ’? And when I approached this 
revelation, His unveiling, it came to me that I never could 
really know Jesus Christ fully but for this unveiling of 
Him. If this was some way to know more about Him, I 
wanted to know it.” He was later to know that the testi- 
mony of Jesus is the very spirit of prophecy, and that 
prophecy is essentially an unveiling of our adorable Lord 
and Savior Jesus Christ. 

Then he read on a little, and he came to the third 
verse, and read: “Blessed is he that readeth.” “Well, 
now,” said he, “it seems I do not have to understand this 
book in order to get some blessing out of it, and if there is 
a blessing to be had from simply reading it, I am going to 
get that blessing.” So he went on and on, reading it and 
studying it and, of course, he became a premillennialist. 
Anybody, in such a case, would become a premillennialist. 


— 348 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


If we could just induce the whole church of God to study 
the book of The Revelation, the whole church would be 
now, as it will be when it gets to heaven, premillennialist. 


In the fifteenth verse of the twenty-fourth chapter of 
Matthew, our Lord Jesus made reference to a prophecy in 
the book of Daniel: “When ye therefore shall see the abom- 
ination of desolation, spoken of by Daniel the prophet, 
stand in the holy place, (whoso readeth, let him under- 
stand).” I call your attention to this to remind you that 
prophecy is to be read and understood, and that there is 
blessing attached both to its reading and to its understand- 
ing. Even in Revelation 1:3, where it says, “Blessed is he 
that readeth,” it goes on to say: “and they that hear the 
words of this prophecy, and keep those things which are 
written therein; for the time is at hand.” No one can prop- 
erly keep the words of this prophecy unless they first un- 
derstand them. 


I want to say, in passing, that in my own experience, 
I have found the study of prophecy not only fascinating 
and delightful, but easy. Prophecy is the most easily un- 
derstood portion of the Word of God. There is that book 
of Daniel, for example; one of the most neglected books in 
the Bible; neglected, I suspect, for the reason that our 
enemy seeks in many ways to keep us from the prophetic 
Word. We have the impression that, while some people, 
maybe, are able (and we doubt even that) to understand 
the book of Daniel, we ourselves certainly are not able to 
understand it. It is like going through the zoological gar- 
dens to us, like traveling through a menagerie of wild beasts 
and strange monsters, full of puzzles, enigmas, etc. I don’t 
blame people for not being interested in puzzles. I never 


Da Too 


GOD HATH SPOKEN. 


have been able to solve them myself. But have you ever 
seriously set yourself to the task of finding what God has 
said in that brief book of Daniel? It is a very fascinating 
book to study, and for the most part it is perfectly easy. 
The Lord tells you something; just paints a picture for us, 
and then sits down with us and tells us what it means— 
the whole thing, generally, in the very chapter itself, so we 
may not only read but understand. We are commanded to 
read them, and we are commanded to understand them, 
and a blessing is promised for so doing. 


3. Then in the third place, we ought to study proph- 
ecy in order to grow in patience and comfort and hope. In 
Romans 15:4 we read: “For whatsoever things were writ- 
ten aforetime were written for our learning, that we 
through patience and comfort of the scriptures might have 
hope.” If you need patience—I mean the kind of patience 
spoken of here, the patience of the Scriptures—and if you 
need the comfort of the Scriptures, and if you need the hope 
based on the patience and the comfort of the Scriptures, I 
advise you to study prophecy, for among the things written 
aforetime for our learning prophecy is included. Prophecy 
is part of the Bible. 


Please turn to Second Timothy, the third chapter. I 
want to call your attention to a test concerning the inspira- 
tion of the Scriptures : “This know also, that in the last days 
perilous times shall come. For men shall be lovers of 
their own selves, covetous, boasters, proud, blasphemers, 
disobedient to parents, unthankful, unholy, without natural 
affection, truce-breakers, false accusers, incontinent, fierce, 
despisers of those that are good, traitors, heady, high- 
minded, lovers of pleasures more than lovers of God; hav- 


— 350 — 


| 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


ing a form of godliness, but denying the power thereof; 
from such turn away” (2 Tim. 3:1-5). 


Five verses. Now let us look at the tenth verse of the 
same chapter: “But thou hast fully known my doctrine, 
manner of life, purpose, faith, longsuffering, charity, pa- 
tience, persecutions, afflictions, which came unto me at An- 
tioch, at Iconium, at Lystra; what persecutions I endured; 
but out of them all the Lord delivered me. Yea, and all 
that will live godly in Christ Jesus shall suffer persecution. 
But evil men and seducers shall wax worse and worse, de- 
ceiving, and being deceived. (Verses 10-13.) 


Now, my friends, that is prophecy. It is prophecy 
even in the limited sense of the word we have before us 
this morning. It is predictive prophecy. Here was a 
preacher, this man Timothy, and the Word of God comes 
to him through the Apostle Paul. Timothy needs to know 
what he is to look for, as he preaches the Word of God. 
Will the people all believe his message? No, evil men 
shall wax worse and worse; but what is this preacher to do 
about it? I read on: “But continue thou in the things 
which thou hast learned and hast been assured of, knowing 
of whom thou hast learned them; and that from a child 
thou hast known the holy scriptures, which are able to 
make thee wise unto salvation through faith which is in 
Christ Jesus. All scripture is given by inspiration of God, 
and is profitable for doctrine, for reproof, for correction, 
for instruction in righteousness” (Verses 14-16). 


Then the fourth chapter, the first verse: “I charge 
thee therefore before God, and the Lord Jesus Christ, who 
shalt judge the quick and the dead at his appearing and his 
kingdom; preach the word; be instant in season, out of 


a SS 


— 351 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


season; reprove, rebuke, exhort with all longsuffering and 
doctrine. For the time will come when they will not endure 
sound doctrine; but after their own lusts shall they heap to 
themselves teachers, having itching ears; and they shall 
turn away their ears from the truth, and shall be turned 
unto fables” (2 Tim. 4: 1-4). 

Now, then, what is he to do about that? “But watch 
thou in all things, endure afflictions, do the work of an 
evangelist, make full proof of thy ministry. For I am 
now ready to be offered, and the time of my departure is at 
hand. I have fought a good fight, I have finished my 
course, I have kept the faith; henceforth there is laid up 
for me a crown of righteousness, which the Lord, the 
righteous judge, shall give me at that day; and not to me 
only, but unto all them also that love his appearing” 
(Verses 5-8). 

You see, do you not, how this schedule given to Tim- 
othy by the Apostle, in the Spirit of God, would prepare 
Timothy for what was surely coming in his path? Why, 
if Timothy had been a postmillennialist—which, of course, 
he was not—then he would have looked for the immediate 
transformation of the city of Ephesus, where he preached, 
and might have looked for the whole city to be converted; 
and, of course, if he had looked for it and had not found 
it, and had worked himself almost to death to accomplish 
that, and did not accomplish it, he would get nervous pros- 
tration. Nervous prostration is the necessary corollary of 
postmillennialism, and, when pressed to its logical sequence, 
will lodge men either in the grave or in the insane asylum. 
It is not that postmillennialists are hypocrites; it is only 
that, not knowing the truth, they are deceived, and are 
looking for that which is not in the program. 


NS SE SRN RS SES SRE SRS 


— 352 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


We are not here to save the world. If we were here 
to save the world, and to save men from their meanness in 
this present dispensation, when we saw we were not getting 
it done we should be discouraged and we should lose all 
hope and go down in despair. The program does not call 
for saving the world at this present time, but to save a peo- 
ple out of the world for the name of the Lord. 


4. In the fourth place, we ought to study prophecy for 
the unfolding of the Scriptures. In Luke 24:25 the Lord 
Jesus calls certain men “fools” because they had not be- 
lieved all that the prophets had spoken. “Ought not Christ 
to have suffered these things, and to enter into his glory? 
And beginning at Moses and all the prophets, he expounded 
unto them in all the scriptures the things concerning him- 
self.” He assured them that all these things should be ful- 
filled, every one of them; and He told them they were 
fools because they had not believed all of them. You can’t 
believe anything you do not know or have not read, and so 
you ought to study prophecy, know prophecy, and thus be 
delivered from that particular foolishness which results 
from neglect of the prophetic Word. 


Notice our Lord’s little key-words—suffering and 
glory. The same key-words are used in 1 Peter 1:11, 
where Peter says that the prophets of old searched dili- 
gently, “Searching what, or what manner of time the Spirit 
of Christ which was in them did signify when it testified 
before-hand the sufferings of Christ, and the glory that 
should follow.” “The sufferings and the glory”’—suffer- 
ings and glory—this is the key. The first advent of Christ 
for suffering; the second advent, for glory. Both are 
prophesied in the Old Testament. Only one so far has 


— 353 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


been fulfilled. But the other is just as sure to be fulfilled 
as God Himself is sure. And, with Paul, “I reckon that 
the sufferings * * * are not worthy to be compared with 
the glory” (Rom. 8:18). It unfolds the Word of God to 
study prophecy. We should never know anything about 
these things but for the Word of prophecy. 


5. Then, in the fifth place, we ought to study the 
Word of prophecy for our own usefulness in God’s service. 
In Second Timothy 2:15 it is written: “Study to shew 
thyself approved unto God, a workman that needeth not to 
be ashamed, rightly dividing the word of truth.” 


Have you no ambition to be useful to God? Would 
you not like to be a skilful workman in the use of His 
Word? You never can be that, until you learn how to 
rightly divide the Word of truth; and you never can learn 
that until you study the Word of truth. And since the 
Word of truth includes very much of prophecy, you must 
read these things and study these things in order to be 
useful to God. Mr. Moody used to have a way, in his 
after-meetings, of going down the aisle with “Are you a 
Christian?“ Now “Are “you a Christians ©. weses 
“Here, you talk to this one.” And he would leave them to- 
gether, the saved one and the unsaved one. But very often 
the poor fellow who was told to talk to his lost neighbor 
did not have a thing to say. It would take him a week to 
find out what to say. Mr. Moody could not talk to all the 
inquirers personally and individually, and so he depended 
on others to talk to them, very often proving that there 
were workmen there who needed to be ashamed because 
they had not studied their Bibles and did not know their 
Book of Regulations—the Word of God.

6. Then, in the sixth place, we ought to study the 
Word of God, the Word of prophecy, in order to be de- 
livered from inexcusable ignorance. Mr. Guille has already 
spoken of the two things in the Epistles of Paul, to which 
attention is called, with reference to our ignorance of 
them. The first is Romans 11:25: “For I would not, 
brethren, that ye should be ignorant of this mystery, lest ye 
should be wise in your own conceits; that blindness in part 
is happened to Israel; until the fulness of the Gentiles be 
come in. And so all Israel shall be saved: as it is written, 
There shall come out of Sion the Deliverer, and shall turn 
away ungodliness from Jacob: For this is my covenant unto 
them, when I shall take away their sins.” 

What a pity that the church of God is so generally and 
so willingly ignorant of God’s purpose concerning the Jews! 
Twenty-five or thirty years ago when I began reading the 
Word of God to folks—reading what God said with refer- 
ence to His purpose concerning the Jewish people—when 
I read to folks that God was going to bring His people back 
to their own land, well, folks used to touch their foreheads 
significantly. The idea of the Jew going back to his own 
land! the idea of Palestine ever again being occupied by the 
Jew! Back in those days no Jews were permittea to enter 
into that land except for a very brief visit and then they 
had to promise to leave it within three months or they 
could not get a passport to go through the borders at all. 
It had been shut to them centuries upon centuries. Folks 
would say, “Do you mean to tell us, us—?” No, I did not 
mean to tell them anything; I was reading to them out of 
the Word of God; but they did not know anything about it, 
except the little bunch here and there. God bless the little 
bunch! You will find them everywhere. And the other 


— 355 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


passage is 1 Thessalonians 4:13: “But I would not have 
you to be ignorant, brethren, concerning them which are 
asleep, that ye sorrow not, even as others which have no 
hope.” 


I am saying to you, we ought to study the Word of 
prophecy in order to be delivered from inexcusable 
ignorance. 


Please turn now to the third chapter of 2 Peter, the 
eighth verse: “But, beloved, be not ignorant of this one 
thing, that one day is with the Lord as a thousand years, 
and a thousand years as one day.” 


I want you to look at the context with me a moment. 
See the first verse: “This second epistle, beloved, I now 
write unto you; in both which I stir up your pure minds by 
way of remembrance: that ye may be mindful of the words 
which were spoken before by the holy prophets, and of the 
commandment of us the apostles of the Lord and Savior: 
knowing this first, that there shall come in the last days 
scoffers, walking after their own lusts, and saying, Where 
is the promise of his coming?” 


They are saying that to us now. They are telling us 
that there is no promise in this Book of His coming. They 
are saying there is no such thing in the Bible as a promise 
of the second coming of Christ. “Where is the promise of 
His Coming?” Of course, they are blind, else they would 
see on almost every page of this Book a promise of His 
coming. 

“For since the fathers fell asleep, all things continue 
as they were from the beginning of the creation. For this 
they willingly are ignorant of, that by the Word of God the 


— 356 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


heavens were of old, and the earth standing out of the 
water and in the water: whereby the world that then was, 
being overflowed with water, perished: but the heavens and 
the earth, which are now, by the same word are kept in 
store, reserved unto fire against the day of judgment and 
perdition of ungodly men. But, beloved, be not ignorant 
of this one thing, that one day is with the Lord as a thous- 
and years, and a thousand years as one day. The Lord is 
not slack concerning his promise, as some men count slack- 
ness; but is longsuffering to us-ward, not willing that any 
should perish, but that all should come to repentance. But 
the day of the Lord will come as a thief in the night; in the 
which the heavens shall pass away with a great noise, and 
the elements shall melt with fervent heat, the earth also and 
the works that are therein shall be burned up.” 


They are “willingly ignorant of this one thing,” that 
there was such a thing one day as the flood of Noah; that 
there was such a thing one day as the fulfillment of an in- 
credible Word of God; that there was such a thing one day 
as the coming upon the earth of a judgment, concerning 
which no one believed, save eight persons only, in the 
whole human race. 


You tell some of these things to people, and they look 
at you and say, “I know about that, but you see, I don’t 
believe it.” And they seem to think that in this way they 
have disposed of the whole subject. “O, well, you know, I 
don’t believe that.” Well, what if you don’t? What has 
that to do with it? Do you suppose that your believing a 
thing makes it true? Or can you dispose of a fact and 
make it false, just by disbelieving it? It is true that very 
much depends on your faith. But if you do not believe, 


on al aera A LE 
— 357 — 


nnd iii, 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


you know what Doctor Haldeman said last night: “If you 
don’t believe, that is your misfortune. It is a gift, like 
music.” O, beloved, it is a great thing if you believe. God 
has given it to you to believe, but don’t go on shutting your 
eyes and then finding fault because you can not see things. 
Get your eyes open. Don’t try to dispose of the Word of 
God by the sin of ignorance. “The day of the Lord will 
come as a thief in the night;” the Word of God has de- 
clared it. 


7. The seventh reason why we ought to study proph- 
ecy is in order that we might be prepared for events. In 
John 16:4 the Lord Jesus is described as speaking a word 
of farewell to His disciples, He has been telling them of 
the things that are to transpire after He leaves them, and 
He says: “But these things have I told you, that when 


the time shall come, ye may remember that I told you of 
them.” 


It is a great thing to know that everything is going on 
according to God’s schedule. It is a great thing to know 
that the purpose of God is not thwarted, is not set aside. 
Is it not a fine thing that He tells us beforehand all about 
it? We are not surprised at the present collapse of civil- 
ization; the Word of God told us all about it. We are not 
surprised that everything in this old world is in a state of 
flux today—everything in society today; that nothing is 
fixed; that the whole world is in unrest. The world is a 
wicked world, and God said, “The wicked are like a 
troubled sea, for it cannot rest, and its waters cast up 
mire and dirt.” We are not looking for roses, where we 
are taught to expect mire and dirt. We are not looking 
for perfection, where we know we shall find only corrup- 


— 358 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


tion. We are not looking for progress, where God told 
us we should see collapse. We are not surprised at things 
as we see them; we are on the train and we are moving 
along the tracks, and we are on time, and we know where 
we get off! 

So, brethren, I commend you to God and to the Word 
of His grace, including the prophetic Word, which is able 
to build you up, and to give you an inheritance among all 
them that are sanctified, 


— 359 — 


THE RESURRECTION 
L. W. Muna tt, D. D. 


Editor of “Eastern Methodist”? Lecturer, Author and 
Bible Teacher. 


The darkest picture that this old, war-scarred, sin- 
cursed world ever saw was Golgotha’s cross. The very 
heavens bowed themselves in midnight mourning and wept. 
The flinty rocks—not so unyielding as your hearts, if you 
are here unsaved this hour—burst in their dumb grief be- 
cause their Maker was put to death by the hands of those 
whom He came to save and to bless. And if the seal that 
was placed on the tomb of Joseph of Arimathaea had re- 
mained unbroken, death would have been to us an endless 
sleep, but now is Christ risen from the dead and become 
the first fruits of them that are sleeping and because He 
lives, we shall live also. 

Let us examine briefly the ground for our confidence 
in this matter. First of all I call your attention to predic- 
tions offered concerning the resurrection of our Lord. In 
the sixteenth Psalm, the tenth verse: “For thou wilt not 
leave my soul in hell; neither wilt thou suffer thine Holy 
One to see corruption.” 

In the second chapter of John, verses 19 and 21, you 
have a prediction made by the Savior Himself: “Destroy 
this temple, and in three days I will raise it up. * * * 
But He spake of the temple of His body.” In the second 
chapter of The Acts of the Apostles you have this testi- 
mony by the Holy Spirit: “Men and brethren, let me 


Helpful books by the above author listed on pages 446-449, 


— 361 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


freely speak unto you of the patriarch David, that he is 
both dead and buried, and his sepulchre'is with us unto 
this day. Therefore, being a prophet, and knowing that 
God had sworn with an oath to him, that of the fruit of 
his loins, according to the flesh, he would raise up Christ to 
sit on his throne * * *.” 

That has never yet taken place. God raised up Christ 
for that purpose and swore by an oath that it should be, 
and since the Strength of Israel cannot lie, it is yet in the 
future and will one day most surely occur. “He seeing this 
before, spake of the resurrection of Christ, that His soul 
was not left in hell, neither His flesh did see corruption. 
This Jesus hath God raised up, whereof we all are wit- 
nesses.” There are some critics of the Bible today who 
deny any Messianic predictions whatever. There are some 
who admit that David did write a very few Psalms, but 
some of them deny that he wrote any. But the Holy Spirit 
of God testifies here that King David wrote that tenth 
verse of the sixteenth Psalm, for He quotes it and declares 
that David wrote it. Therefore, he did, and without doubt 
wrote the rest of that Psalm. Now, these critics will tell 
you that prediction is not prophecy. That the prophet is 
limited in his work to exposition, but the testimony of the 
Holy Ghost here is that David was a “foreseer ;” therefore, 
a predicter, and that this prediction was concerning the 
resurrection of our Lord. 

In the fifteenth chapter of First Corinthians we read: 
“For I delivered unto you first of all that which I also re- 
ceived, how that Christ died for our sins according to the 
scriptures.” What Scriptures, pray? The New Testament 
Scriptures were not in existence when Paul penned these 
words—possibly Galatians, the Thessalonian Epistles, and 


— 362 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


a portion of the Gospel of Matthew, were already written. 
Therefore, according to the Scriptures means the OLD 
TESTAMENT SCRIPTURES, “and that he was buried 
and rose again the third day, according to the scriptures’”— 
the Old Testament predictive Scriptures, of course. 

“And that he was seen of Cephas, then of the twelve. 
After that, he was seen of above five hundred brethren at 
once; of whom the greater part remain unto this present, 
but some are fallen asleep. After that, he was seen of 
James; then of all the apostles. And last of all he was 
seen of me also, as of one born out of due time.” 

Authorities on testimony are uniformly agreed that 
there is no fact more clearly established than the resurrec- 
tion of Jesus Christ. Babbage in his Bridgewater treatise 
shows by mathematical calculations that if there are twenty- 
five men who are known to tell the truth ten times as 
often as they lie, and they testify concerning any matter of 
sufficient importance to make them careful in their testi- 
mony, and they agree in that testimony, there are a billion 
chances to one that it is the truth. Now, here are more 
than 500 reputable, competent witnesses who testify con- 
cerning the most important fact of all history, only one of 
whom was ever known to lie, and he only at one time, and 
their testimony is in substantial agreement; therefore, it 
amounts to what the mathematicians call a “demonstra- 
tion.” And you know anything that is mathematically 
demonstrated to be correct is true. 

I suppose, of course, therefore, you all believe in the 
resurrection of our Lord and Savior, Jesus Christ. You 
would be infidels if you did not, and none of you is an in- 
fidel, I presume—yet one may have strayed in here; we are 
glad if he is here—but the members of this Conference do 


— 363 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


not belong to that company. But are you sure now that 
you do believe in this matter, for His resurrection from the 
dead is the very heart of this whole system that we call 
Christianity? 

Let me put you to the test: I have met a few people 
who truly believe, they say, in the resurrection of Jesus 
Christ from the dead, and yet when they were put to the 
test they were forced to admit they did not. Do you be- 
lieve in the literal, “sure-enough”’—as they say down in 
Dixie land—resurrection of the bodies of the saints of 
God at the second coming of our Lord? Do you? There 
are so many theories and so many speculations as to that, 
and some are plausible, and they capture a great many peo- 
ple who are not wise enough to search the Scriptures to see 
whether these things are so and prove thereby that which 
is worth holding. I mean a literal, real resurrection of 
these mortal bodies, because the word “resurrection” means 
that and it means nothing else. 

I want to read you something from one who has a 
good deal of influence even in these days. The Wesleys 
were poets, all of them. Charles Wesley wrote more than 
twice as many hymns as anybody besides ever did, and not 
one of them is a poor hymn, for they are Scriptural, and 
more than 5,000 of them are millennial hymns and a great 
many of them clear-cut premillennial hymns. John Wesley 
was given to administration matters and did not get much 
chance to exercise his poetic talent, but here is a hymn by 
John Wesley himself: 


I call the Lord’s Redeemer mine; 

He lives Who died for me I know— 
Who bought my soul with blood divine, 
Jesus shall re-appear below, 

Stand in that dreadful day unknown, 
And fix on earth His Heavenly throne.
