S 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Then the last judgment day shall come, 
And tho’ the worms this skin devour, 
The Judge shall call me from the tomb, 
Shall bid the greedy grave restore, 
And raise this individual me, 

God in the flesh, my God to see. 


In this identic body, I, 

With eyes of flesh refined, restored, 
Shall see that self-same Savior nigh; 
See for myself my smiling Lord; 
See with ineffable delight; 

Nor faint to bear the glorious sight. 


Then let the worms demand their pray, 
The greedy grave my reins consume; 
With joy I drop my mouldering clay, 
And rest till my Redeemer come; 

On Christ my life in death rely, 

Secure that I can never die. 


This is good biblical theology—it is surely according 
to the Scriptures. Here is the testing: Let me put you to 
it. “If Christ be preached that he arose from the dead, 
how say some among you that there is no resurrection of 
the dead? But if there be no resurrection of the dead, 
then is Christ not risen: And if Christ be not risen, then 
is our preaching vain, and your faith is also vain. Yea, 
and we are found false witnesses of God; because we have 
testified of God that he raised up Christ: Whom he raised 
mor up it 1s0, besthat. the déad rise not. A A 0 *"" Kor 
* * * if Christ be not raised, your faith is vain.” No 
man can deny the resurrection of God’s people and con- 
sistently say Jesus was raised from the dead. 


“JT take it for granted, therefore, that you are all right 
about this matter, and now I want your attention directly 
to the resurrection of the saints. The most learned men on 


po 


— 365 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


earth know nothing about it apart from the testimony of 
the sacred Scriptures. Human opinion is absolutely worth- 
less and would not be admitted in any court on earth, and 
can’t be admitted at this Conference. Where have we any 
information that is trustworthy, and therefore, dependa- 
ble, and that can make us here, pilgrims of a day, where 
the shadows lie long and dark across our pathway, rejoice 
in hope? I say to you that not anywhere is there any 
trustworthy evidence apart from the testimony of the sa- 
cred Scriptures themselves. I know that in the natural 
world, and in the domain of science, you can find illustra- 
tions of this truth. 

The budding life of springtime is suggestive. I could 
take from your finger the gold ring you are wearing, and 
dropping it into certain acids it would soon disappear, 
though held in solution. Then I could drop into that solu- 
tion certain salts and precipitate that gold and mould it 
into a ring and put it back on your finger. That beauti- 
fully illustrates the doctrine of the resurrection. Or, more 
beautiful still, take that repulsive, defenseless worm, that 
spins its own winding sheet and for a season lies inanimate, 
and then that winding sheet bursts and that worm no 
longer repulsive comes forth a beautiful moth or butterfly 
that wings itself away on the free air of heaven. But 
what say the Scriptures? “To the law and to the testimony; 
if they speak not according to this word, it is because there 
is no light in them.” . 

Let us go back to the sixteenth Psalm and read the 
ninth verse: “Therefore is my heart glad, and my glory 
rejoiceth; my flesh also shall rest in hope.” David saw an 
open grave lying across his pathway and knew that he was 
certainly going into it, but by the revelation given of God 


SS 


== G00 <= 


a ED 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


his faith overleaped the thousand years that lay between, 
and he saw his Lord and Savior descend into the grave 
and, grappling with death subdue him; and breaking the 
bars of the grave, arise victoriously therefrom, leading cap- 
tivity captive and giving gifts unto men and King David 
went into the grave, as multitudes of saints have since 
gone, with a shout of victory. But, it was his flesh that 
went into the grave—his natural, mortal body. 

Will you turn please to Daniel 12:2: “And many 
from among the sleepers in the dust of the earth shall 
awake, these that first awake, shall be unto everlasting 
life, and the rest, when they awake, shall be unto shame 
and everlasting contempt.” (Tregelles.) The Bible no- 
where teaches a general resurrection. “Many” does not 
include ALL. What is it that is in the dust of the earth? 
Nothing but the body. The Bible nowhere teaches soul 
sleeping as some allege—the unconscious state of the dead 
in the middle stage. It is the body that sleeps in the dust 
of the earth. At the death of the Christian the spirit re- 
turns to God who gave it. That is God’s Word: and, the 
soul departs to be with Christ, absent from the body, 
present with the Lord, conscious in happiness and felicity, 
and the body goes into the grave until the time for its res- 
urrection, at the second coming of Christ, Who returning 
brings those absent from the body with Him, when soul, 
body and spirit are united and the work of salvation fin- 
ished. 

In the case of unchristian persons, they have no spir- 
itual life; and so when the Bible says the spirit returns to 
the God who gave it, it refers to those who have spiritual 
existence. The unchristian soul is immortal and when it 
leaves the body it goes to the place of punishment and tor- 


— 367 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


ment, as in the situation set forth in the sixteenth chapter 
of Luke. Therefore, the unsaved need to be born again, 
or they never can enter the kingdom of God. But it is the 
body that is given into dust and earth. 

Come, if you please, to the fifth chapter of John, 
verses 28 and 29: “Marvel not at this: for the hour is 
coming, in the which all that are in the grave shall hear his 
voice, and shall come forth; they that have done good, unto 
the resurrection of life; and they that have done evil, unto 
the resurrection of damnation.” The “hour” there in the 
context means all the time that has elapsed since the words 
fell from our Savior’s lips and that will yet elapse until He 
comes to assert His Kingly rights. Two resurrections, as 
in the previous passage quoted. 

What is it that is in the grave? Nothing but the body. 
Therefore, if the body does not come from the grave, 
there is no resurrection at all, “And the sea shall give up 
its dead,” and pray, what is it that is in the sea but the 
body. Therefore if it is not the body that comes forth 
there is no resurrection at all. 

Turn to the eighth chapter of Romans: “But if the 
spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead dwell in 
you, he that raised up Christ from the dead shall also 
quicken your mortal bodies by his spirit that dwelleth in 
you.” (Rom. 8:11). Who raised Jesus from the dead? 
The Holy Spirit. He is the executive of the Godhead in 
all the work of God from creation to the culmination of 
the kingdom. 

In the third chapter of 1 Peter, verse 18, we are told 
Jesus “also hath once suffered for sins, the just for the un- 
just, that he might bring us to God, being put to death in 
the flesh, but quickened by the Spirit.” Now, if the Holy 


— 368 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Ghost that raised Jesus Christ from the dead is in us, that 
is God’s confirmation of His pledge to raise us from the 
dead. 

In the first chapter of Ephesians, thirteenth verse, 
“after ye believed, ye were sealed with that holy Spirit of 
promise.” What is a seal? A seal is that which distin- 
guishes a bill or document as valid. Take a $10 green- 
back. You might have a bill like that printed on a govern- 
ment press and signed by government officials, but if the 
seal is not there it is worth nothing; hand it to the cashier 
and he will hand it back to you as worthless, for that seal 
is the government’s pledge to redeem that bill with the coin 
that it represents; otherwise it is worthless. 

A body may belong to church down here and go 
through the forms of worship and do a great many very 
nice things that even might shame some folks that ought to 
do better, but if any man have not the spirit of God, while 
he could pass here he could not pass at the chancery yon- 
der. So it is an important question, “Have you received 
the Holy Ghost since ye have believed?” “Which is the 
earnest of our inheritance, until the redemption of the pur- 
chased possession.” That word “earnest” is a legal term 
and means first payment. If I were to bargain with you 
for a piece of property and we were to come to terms and 
I handed you $10, the minute you received that $10 that 
property becomes mine in law, though the conveyance has 
not been made. The earnest money seals the bargain. 
This gift of the Holy Ghost is the earnest of God’s pledge 
that what is promised as to resurrection will be fulfilled. 
“That by two immutable things, in which it was impossible 
for God to lie, we might have a strong consolation, who 
have fled for refuge to lay hold upon the hope set before 


— 369 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


us. Which hope we have as an anchor of the soul, both 
sure and stedfast, and which entereth into that within the 
veil; whither the forerunner is for us entered, even Jesus, 
made an high priest forever after the order of Melchisedec.” 
If you have the seal, the earnest, of the Holy Spirit, you 
have God’s solemn covenant and pledge for the resurrec- 
tion of your body, if you shall pass from time to eternity 
before Jesus comes. 

Now, then, come along please to the fifteenth chapter 
of First Corinthians.. I listened to a very able preacher 
sometime ago preaching a sermon on the resurrection. His 
text was “It is sown a natural body; it is raised a spiritual 
body,” and, he had what he called his egg-skin theory. He 
said, if you take an egg and crack it carefully, you can re- 
move the shell and inside there is a film that holds the egg. 
That conforms in its convexity to the concavity of the 
shell. I happened to be old enough to know that that 
preacher made that sermon to fit his preconceived theory, 
and then hunted up a text to put on it. Analyze the word 
and you will find the text contradicts his theory altogether, 
for it definitely states that that which is sown is that which 
is raised. Come along to the Philippian Epistle: “For our 
conversation is in heaven; from whence also we look for 
the Savior, the Lord Jesus Christ; who shall change our 
vile body, that it may be fashioned like unto his glorious 
body, according to the working whereby he is able even to 
subdue all things unto himself.” (Phil. 3:20, 21.) The 
word used there is the word from which we get our word 
“politics” and the revised version has changed it to “citi- 
zenship”—“our citizenship is in heaven.” The church of 
God is an heavenly people and we are down here for the 
business the Lord has assigned to us and we ought to be at 


— 370 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


it all the time. It is the mortal body that is changed, ac- 
cording to the declaration of the Word of God. 

Now in the context in that third chapter of Philip- 
pians, Paul said he was willing to fellowship Christ in His 
sufferings “If by any means I might attain unto the resur- 
rection of the dead.” The scholars are all agreed that 
properly rendered it should be the resurrection from among 
the dead. Literally—the out resurrection from among the 
dead. If everybody is going to rise at one and the same 
time he would get up anyhow; but Paul knew that blessed 
and holy are they that have part in the first resurrection, 
over such the second death should have no power. The 
glories of that day were so great as to dazzle him. The 
horrors of the second resurrection he also knew, and know- 
ing he was willing to fellowship Christ in suffering, if nec- 
essary and possible, to attain the one and escape the other— 
and he came nearer doing it than any man who ever trod 
the earth, humanly speaking. Now look at First Thessa- 
lonians 4:13-18: “I would not have you ignorant, breth- 
ren, concerning them that are asleep, that ye sorrow not, 
even as others which have no hope. For if we believe that 
Jesus died and rose again, even so them also which sleep in 
Jesus will God bring with Him. For this we say unto you 
by the Word of the Lord, that we which are alive and re- 
main unto the coming of the Lord, shall not prevent (go 
before) them which are asleep. For the Lord Himself 
shall descend from heaven with a shout, with the voice of 
the archangel, and with the trump of God; and the dead in 
‘Christ shall rise first: then we which are alive and remain, 
shall be caught up together with them in the clouds, to 
meet the Lord in the air: and so shall we ever be with the 
‘Lord. Wherefore comfort (exhort) one another with

GOD HATH SPOKEN 


these words.” I once read an editorial, by a friend of 
mine, on “Dogmatism.” He said this: “In view of the 
fact that we know so few things to be absolutely true, we 
ought to be careful not to dogmatize. If I say two and 
two make four, I may dogmatize about that because I know 
they do. Or, if I shall say ‘All men will die,’ I may dog- 
matize about that, for that is absolutely true.” It is abso- 
lutely not true. This Scripture plainly teaches that the last 
generation of Christians in this age will not die but be 
translated—“changed, in a moment, in the twinkling of an 
eye at the last trump.” “We shall not all sleep.” This cor- 
ruptible (dead ones) must put on incorruption, and this 
mortal (living ones) must put on immortality. If we are 
His, it does not matter “Whether we wake or sleep we 
should live together with Him.” If Jesus were to come 
this very hour we would not go through the grave, but 
through the clouds of heaven. Jesus said to the sorrowing 
sisters of Lazarus, “I am the resurrection and the life; he 
that believeth in me, though he were dead, yet shall he 
live; and whosoever liveth and believeth in me shall never 
die.” I do not like the idea of going into the grave. My 
body has served me a good many years; and I don’t like 
the idea of it going down into rottenness; I do not get time 
to think much about that and so do not bother about it, for 
I have been delivered from the bondage of death, and can 
say “O, death: where is thy sting?” Death is like the 
honey-bee—it never stings but once. Death lost its sting 
forever to those who believe in Him, when he drove it into 
the kingly heart of our risen Lord. Moses’ body was taken 
care of by God when he died; Elijah was taken up by the 
whirlwind into heaven, and they two were in the mount of 
transfiguration, which is a fore-picture of the end of the

age, Christ glorified with risen and translated saints. They 
two were also at the sepulchre the first Easter morning, 
and they two were on the mount of ascension. 

If the dead rise not, then you must teach the physical 
annihilation of the individual when Jesus comes. That 
would be monstrous, and not the “blessed hope.” 

One other passage, and that is this: In the First Epis- 
tle of John 3:2: “Now are we the sons of God, and it

when he shall appear, we shall be like him; for we shall see

born of a woman, born under the law, so the Bible tells us, 
as you and I were born of our mothers, in a physical body; 
and, according to the same physical laws that our bodies 
have been developed from babyhood, his body was devel- 
oped from the babe of Bethlehem to the perfect physical 
man put upon the cross of Calvary. The life went out 
from that body and it was laid in the tomb of Joseph of 
Arimathaea, and the mouth of that tomb was closed with a 
stone and upon it was put the Roman seal, which meant 
death to anybody that broke it and a guard of soldiers was 
put there to watch. But on the morning of the third day 
that seal was broken and the stone rolled away, and the 
women saw Jesus and knew Him and would have held Him 
by the feet and worshipped Him, but He said: “Touch 
me not; for I am not yet ascended to my father; but go to 
my brethren, and say unto them, I ascend unto my Father.” 
And that first Easter morning Christ ascended to heaven 
and offered His blood that had been shed for the ransom- 
ing of the world, on the altar where it speaks now, and has 
spoken ever since in behalf of all those who have followed 
Him in the regeneration. He returned shortly afterwards

and meeting those women again allowed them to touch Him

Whenever the priest shed the blood of the sin offering 
he must take it into the Holy of Holies and sprinkle it on 
the altar between the two outstretched wings of the sera- 
phim; and the ceremonial law forbade a woman touching 
the priest after the blood was shed, and before it was of- 
fered in the Holy of Holies. Jesus Christ was the priest 
and He was the sin offering too, for He shed His own 
blood; and “He was subject to the law in all things,” and 
therefore would not allow the women to touch Him until 
He had ascended to the Father and offered that blood on 
the altar where it speaks prevailingly for us, who are fled 
for refuge to lay hold on the hope set before us. 

Now, the disciples heard about it and Thomas did not 
believe and the Master appeared in their midst, the door 
being shut. This is claimed by some, who are skeptically 
inclined, to be “indisputable” evidence that Christ did not 
have that same body. But you remember that Christ dis- 
appeared and reappeared even before his death on the 
cross from the presence of the people. The late Professor 
Christies once said: “The resurrection body of Christ was 
not altered as to its component parts. It was the same 
body of flesh and bones, but in resurrection life standing 
under other laws.” I could not go through that wall with 
this body of mine if there were not an opening large 
enough to permit of its passage; but when I get my resur- 
rection body you will not be able to fence me in; I will 
have the range of the universe even as my Lord had. 
Knowing what Thomas had said, He said: “Thomas, 
reach hither thy finger, and behold my hands, that was

once the baby hand that my mother used to fondle as

expanded and grew until it became a full-grown man’s 
hand; you know how many times it raised the fallen, wiped

and the needy and pointing with unerring accuracy the way 
of life and hope and heaven. Come, Thomas, behold the 
spear-thrust and handle me and see, for a spirit hath not 
flesh and bones as you see me have.” 

Jesus was seen eleven times subsequent to His resur- 
rection; He walked with the disciples, and talked with 
them and even ate food in that resurrection body, and now 
He must needs ascend to the Father’s presence to begin His 
priestly advocacy, and it was most natural that He should 
go in the direction He went. 

He was misunderstood; so have you been misunder- 
stood if you have been living as you ought to; and that is 
never pleasant. He was lied about more than anybody who 
ever trod this earth; so have you been lied about if you 
have been true to Him. He said “The servant is not 
greater than his Lord,” these things did they unto Him and 
they will do them unto you. O, how His heart used to

would not have Him to reign over them. They

the heart of our Lord. O, the depths of His agony as He 
looked over the city and wept and cried: “O, Jerusalem,

Jerusalem, which killest the prophets, and stonest them

The late Bishop Merrill of the Methodist Church,

no coming at all.” 

He will come with His glorified body—and when

as He went away, and we shall be like Him for we shall

her say: “I wonder if anybody will think enough of me 
when I am gone to do that for me.” Remembering this 
as I was about to visit her grave for the first time, I went

well carry, and took them to the cemetery and strewed

mother’s grave. It was a beautiful autumnal evening

this; whatever else does this old war-scarred, sin-cursed 
world need so much? Go from this place to tell it out, 
He is the resurrection and the life and we will live again 
and reign with Him in majesty and dominion.

THE CENTRAL THEME OF SCRIPTURE 
PROPHECY—THE RETURN 


GrorceE McNEELy 


Pastor Elizabeth Avenue Baptist Church, Elizabeth, N. J. 


The matter to be set forth in support of the proposi- 
tion that the central theme of Scripture Prophecy is “The 
Return of Our Lord” will be developed from that which 
is contained in the 15th chapter of The Acts, verses 14-16. 
“First God did visit the Gentiles to take out of them a 
people for His name, and to this agree the words of the 
prophets; as it is written: After these things I will return.” 

In verse 28 of this 15th chapter it is declared that 
God the Holy Spirit was with them in their deliberations 
at this first Church Council. All their conclusions were 
therefore formed by the Holy Spirit, and set forth in 
words which the Holy Spirit taught. 

This was the office of the Holy Spirit as God the Son 
informed the disciples, saying, “When he the Spirit of 
truth is come he will guide you into all truth: for he 
shall not speak of himself; but whatsoever he shall hear 
that shall he speak; and he will show you things to come.” 
In the course of His office the Holy Spirit showed the 
disciples the true interpretation of Jeremiah 12: 14-16. 
Therefore James could stand up and voice the sentiment 
of the Church toward those who from among the Gentiles 
turned to God, and declare that the words of the prophets 
harmonized with the salvation of the Gentiles on the one 
condition of belief in the Lord Jesus Christ; and all pre- 


— 379 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


dicted events centered in the expected glorious return of 
the Lord and Savior. 


The importance of prophecy is indicated by the fact 
that it was used at the first Council of the church as a 
standard of agreement, and that with which it did not agree 
was to be rejected by the people of God. 

The prophetic Word spoken in the past by men or- 
dained of God is more sure than a message in the present 
no matter how eminent the one who speaks. Peter calls 
attention to this fact in the first chapter of his second 
epistle, stating that the disciples heard a voice when they 
were with the Lord on the Mount of Transfiguration. 
Nevertheless, though they heard this voice, they leaned 
rather on the more sure word of prophecy. The Lord Him- 
self intimates the excellence of the prophetic word in 
Luke 16. It is there stated in relation to the matter of 
eternal life or eternal death: “If they believe not Moses 
and the prophets neither would they be persuaded though 
one rose from the dead,” and spake to them. This ex- 
cellence of the prophetic Word in appealing to men lies in 
the fact that it has been tested by the experience of human- 
ity through thousands of years; and not one of the children 
of men who trusted it has been confounded. 


The word spoken in the present must be tested by time, 
as indicated in Deut. 18:22. God declares Himselt as “The 
Only One” who can “Show things to come to pass, and 
declare things to come” (Isaiah 41: 21-23). 


The strength of the proof by time, and experience as 
to the reliability of Bible prophecy is the more wonderful 
because prophets in the Bible not only set forth in outline 
the course of events; but in a minute manner described 
Ee ere 


— 380 — 


o te ape ye 
E 


Ls S 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the events. From fulfilled prophecy we take the illustra- 
tion of the birth of Christ. The wise men from the East 
having inquired of King Herod “Where is he that is born 
King of the Jews?” the priests of Israel can supply the 
information from the words of the prophets, giving the 
land, the tribe to which it belonged, the name of the village, 
belonging to that tribe and the character of the village 
(Matthew 2). Likewise the prophets have foretold the 
coming of a league of nations under ten kings, who shall 
unite and elect an emperor. That the emperor thus elected 
by the nations will proceed to the Temple of God, and 
boast that he is God, attempting thus to usurp the preroga- 
tives of Deity. That he shall have a mark by which when 
he arrives at the Temple of God, Jews who have studied 
the prophetic Word will be able to recognize him. His 
end is to be destruction at the coming of the Lord, who 
will then establish His kingdom over every nation under 
heaven. (Dan. 7:27; Matt. 25: 31-46; 2nd Thess. 2: 5-12; 
Rev. 13.) Events now falling upon the earth display the 
beginnings of these things. Thus it is made more sure 
to us regarding prophecy, that “The mouth of the Lord 
hath spoken it.” David being a prophet declared “The 
Spirit of the Lord spake by me and his word was in my 
mouth.” This statement of David’s is of one piece with 
that of the apostle Peter who tells us that “The Word of 
God came not in old time by the will of man, but holy 
men of God spake as they were moved by the Holy Spirit.” 
God the Holy Spirit who moved the prophets to write 
came in power upon the apostles also and enabled them to 
interpret and make a correct application of the things 
which He the Holy Spirit caused to be written before. 
Which things—in their inspired application—are now re- 


— 381 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


ported unto us by them who preached the Gospel unto us 
with the Holy Spirit sent down from Heaven. 

In the 18th chapter of Deuteronomy, Moses declares 
to Israel, “A Prophet shall the Lord your God raise up 
unto you like unto me, Him shall ye hear.” The statement 
that the promised one would be like Moses does not imply 
inferiority, but superiority, as the context proves. It was 
requested by Israel that God should not speak to them vis- 
ibly but veil Himself in the form of man, thus they might 
hear and see God and yet live. God therefore said unto 
Moses “I will raise them up a Prophet from among their 
brethren like unto thee, and will put my words in his 
mouth; and he shall speak unto them all that I shall com- 
mand him. And it shall come to pass that whosoever will 
not hearken unto my words which he shall speak in my 
name, I will require it of him.” Peter quotes this passage 
in Acts 3 where it is rendered “Every soul which will not 
hear that Prophet shall be destroyed from among the peo- 
ple.” The statement “I will require it of him” for failing to 
hear, being equivalent to I will destroy him for failing to 
hear. The tremendous importance of hearing that Prophet 
gives added meaning to the Lord’s question, “Whom do 
men say that I the Son of Man am?” The reply that He 
was taken to be but one of the prophets, not The Prophet, 
explains their fear on the Mount of Transfiguration where 
they fell on their faces and were sore afraid because of the 
voice from heaven, which said, “This is my beloved Son, 
in whom I am well pleased, hear ye him.” The startling 
words “Hear ye Him” brought home to them the crisis in 
which they stood. The One with whom they had been 
on such familiar terms, whose call “Follow Me!” had been 
unheard and unheeded by so many, and only indifferently 


SS 


— 382 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


heeded by themselves, was that prophet Whom not to hear 
meant destruction. No wonder they were sore afraid and 
needed His touch and word “Fear not.” No wonder the 
mighty Paul cries to the Hebrews saying, “God who at 
sundry times and in divers manners spake in time past 
unto the fathers by the Prophets hath in these last days 
spoken unto us by His Son.” “Therefore we ought to 
give the more earnest heed to the things which we have 
heard, * * * for if the word spoken by messengers was 
steadfast, and every disobedient person received a due 
punishment, how shall we escape destruction if we neglect 
the great salvation spoken by the Lord and confirmed 
unto us by them that heard Him?” 

The Lord Jesus Christ was Himself the Great 
Prophet. All others but sounded forth His words. It is 
so declared in their formulas, such as “The word of the 
Lord came unto me.” “The Lord spake by me, and His 
word was in my mouth.” 

The Lord Himself as the source of all prophecy di- 
vided it into two sections, namely: that which concerned 
His sufferings and that concerning His glory which should 
follow. (Luke 24:25-27). 

All that the Lord prophesied concerning His sufferings, 
beginning by His own mouth (Gen. 3:15) and continuing 
through all the prophets that followed, He hath so fulfilled. 
(Acts 3:18.) When Christ triumphantly called heaven 
and earth to witness that He had finished His work on 
the cross of Calvary, He changed that great section of the 
Word of God which concerned His sufferings from matters 
of prophecy into matters of fact. Therefore, all that now 
remains of prophecy concerns the glory of Christ and if 
we are shown that the glory of Christ is bound up with 


— 383 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the return of Christ we must conclude that the central 
theme of prophecy is “The Return of The Lord.” 

The power conferred upon the disciples at Pentecost 
enabled them to apply the prophetic Word. Therefore, 
whenever we find a New Testament application of Old 
Testament prophecy we regard it as authoritative. The 
New Testament by this means clearly shows the glory of 
the Lord to be inseparably united with the return of the 
Lord. 

It has been objected, to the method of Scripture proof 
which we shall use, that it degrades the Bible into being a 
magazine of proof texts. The fact remains, however, that 
those who object to the “proof text method” object to such 
use, not because they would not use Scripture texts to 
prove their own interpretations, true, but because Scripture 
does not lend itself to prove their interpretations. “The 
things of God knoweth no man, but the Spirit of God” and 
the Spirit of God in establishing New Testament doctrine 
does so by massing Old Testament texts. The “proof text 
method” is therefore the method of the Spirit. 

Peter calls the holy Prophets as witnesses to the fact 
that all their denunciations upon the wickednesses of the 
world will have fulfillment by the Lord’s return in judg- 
ment and in that return will be the glory (2nd Peter 3: 
connecting verses 2, 3, 18). 

James seizes upon the great symptom of wickedness: 
the sufferings brought upon men by capital and labor. He -< 
declares that this iniquity exists because men have not faith 
in the glory of the Lord, that the Prophets had this faith 
and declared the glory to be the return of the Lord. 

Paul in his epistles to the Corinthians makes more 
than thirty applications of Old Testament Scripture, sum-

a Si 


SA LS 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 
E 


ming up their teaching to be glory for us. This glory will 
be imparted in resurrection (1 Cor. 15:43). The resur- 
rection and glory to be by the return of the Lord. (1 Cor. 
15:23). l 

In Matthew 19:28 the Lord informed His disciples 
of a time when they should sit upon twelve thrones. 
Matthew 25:31 shows that time to be when He Himself 
should sit upon the throne of His glory at His return. 
Matthew 19:28 connects the Lord’s glory with a time of 
regeneration. In Acts 3:20, 21 Peter connects the regenera- 
tion of things and the glory with the return of the Lord. 
The last testimony of the Lord Himself to the Nation as 
a whole and to the disciples as chosen to bear witness of 
the fact, was that He would return in glory. (Matt. 
24:30; 26:64; Mark 13:26; Luke 21:27; John 14:1-3; 
Acts Ery 3 

Now of the things set forth in the foregoing words 
this is the sum: the word of prophecy is sure and steadfast. 
The Lord Himself being The Prophet. All prophecy now 
relates to the glory of the Lord. The glory of the Lord 
and the return of the Lord are both of one piece. There- 
fore, having established these things from Scripture, we 
have before us with all the strength of a mathematical 
demonstration the fact that the central theme of prophecy 
is the return of the Lord. 

The return of our Lord being the great subject of 
prophecy, it is important that we carefully distinguish be- 
tween what it is to set forth the prophecies of the Lord’s 
return and prophesying as to what His return will be like. 
The former is proclaiming what “the mouth of the Lord 
has spoken,” (and there is no subject having the attention 
of men about which they are in such agreement as are the 


— 385 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


students of prophecy regarding the message of the 
prophets). The latter is proclaiming the judgments of 
man and gives room for much disagreement. 

For the Christian, the sphere of prephecy would seem 
to be indicated in the Lord’s saying “Now I have told you 
before it come to pass, that when it is come to pass, ye 
might believe.” Not believe to the saving of their souls, 
as the context proves; but believe to the comfort of their 
hearts. As they saw some of the things spoken of come to 
pass, they became assured that “All that the Lord hath 
spoken He would do.” 

In eomxection with the Lord’s return, things may be 
the more clearly grasped if looked at according to the sug- 
gested divisions of 1 Cor. 10:32, Jew, Gentile, and the 
church of God. 

In connection with the church, the Lord’s return will 
be to call her to meet Him in the air. The Lord Himself 
declares it to be so, and commissioned Paul to inform the 
church that He Himself would descend from heaven with 
a shout—with a shout he came to the grave of Lazarus. 
Then He called Lazarus only, but this time all. “The dead 
in Christ shall rise first, then we which are alive and remain 
shall be caught up together with the resurrected ones, to 
meet the Lord in the air.” This is the teaching of the New 
Testament, and it is illustrated by events from the Old 
Testament. “Enoch walked with God and was not, for 
God took him.” “Elijah was taken up to be with the 
Lord without dying.” Peter in his Second Epistle 1: 15-18 
makes the Mount of Transfiguration to be a sample of 
the coming of our Lord. There, in the glory which shed 
its radiance on disciples below, there appeared with the 
Lord in His glory, Moses a type of the resurrected saints, 
ee ee ee ee 

— 386 — 


a a 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 


and Elijah a type of the saints taken up to glory without 
dying. It is by being thus caught up to meet the Lord 
coming in His glory, that it shall come to pass with be- 
lievers, as it is written in Col. 3:4 “When Christ who is 
our life shall appear, then shall ye also appear with Him 
in glory.” The Holy Spirit through Jude assures us, that 
it was of this occasion Enoch the seventh from Adam, 
prophesied, when he declared “The Lord cometh with ten 
thousands of His saints, to execute judgment upon all.” 
Between this moment and the moment of the church being 
caught up to meet the Lord in the air, not one thing need 
take place beside the completion of the church body. (Eph. 
4:13.) The church body is now being completed by the 
operation of God taking out of the world a people for 
His Name, Acts 15:14. This operation of God’s is 
referred to in a form of sound words that is contained in 
the Protestant Episcopal ritual of burial. “We beseech 
thee shortly to accomplish the number of thine elect, and 
to hasten thy kingdom; that we (the living) with all those 
that are departed in the true faith of thy Holy Name, may 
have our perfect consummation and bliss, both in body and 
soul, in thy eternal and everlasting glory.” 

Name implies nature. Adam gave all things a name 
according to their nature. The name of Abram, Jacob, 
Simon, Bar-Jona, was changed with a change in their nature. 
God changes our nature. We are born of the Spirit when 
we believe God. “God gives to us exceeding precious 
promises whereby we are made partakers of the divine 
nature,” (2 Peter 1:4). 

_ We are constituted a people for His Name by being 
of His nature, and being of His nature and name, we are 
fit to rule. Therefore the Lord will have us with Him to 


— 387 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


rule in His kingdom and glory. Then will be fulfilled the 
promise, “The saints shall judge the world.” (1 Cor. 
6: 1-3.) 

During the time beginning with Pentecost and ending 
with the return of the Lord, it is foretold that there will 
be great apostasy. Numbers shall depart from the faith. 
Whole congregations, it is indicated, will turn away from 
the faith, and put their trust in uncertain riches. Saying: 
“We are rich and increased with goods and have need of 
nothing”—thus rejecting the true office of every believer, 
to witness unto a risen, ascended, and returning Lord and 
Savior. On every side we see the evidence of this con- 
dition. Its coming is increasing in speed until it shall come 
as a mighty torrent, sweeping all truth and godliness before 
it. But though “The floods lift up their voice,” God who 
is mightier than they shall command, “Thus far and no 
further, here shall thy proud waves be stayed.” “When 
the enemy shall come in like a flood, then shall the Lord 
lift up a standard against him, and to this agree the words 
of the prophets, as it is written, After this I will Return.” 

The return of the Lord in relation to Jew and Gentile, 
is clearly indicated in the fourth chapter of the book of 
The Acts, beginning with the 23rd verse, where the com- 
pany of believers inspired by God, make application of 
the 2nd Psalm. Showing that it was not alone the Jews 
who crucified the Lord. Jew and Gentile together were 
united in transgression. “For of a truth against thy Holy 
Child Jesus, whom thou hast anointed, both Herod, and 
Pontius Pilate, with the Gentiles and the people of Israel 
were gathered together.” This gathering together against 
the Lord has been a continuous course of the natural man. 
In the eleventh chapter of Genesis, we have a gathering of 


SSE ES A RR ENR ART RE SR AEE 


— 388 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the people against the Lord. The Lord came down and 
judged them. In the language of the 6th verse this rebellion 
against the Lord was begun at Babel. It was continued at 
Calvary, and will be ended at Armageddon, by the return 
of the Lord and all His saints with him. (Rev. 16: 13-16; 
19: 11-21.) 

In Matthew 25: 31-34 the return of the Lord in glory 
is spoken of by Himself. At that time He will say to those 
nations which are given the “Right Hand” place, “Come 
ye blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom prepared for 
you from the foundation of the world.” Mark that the 
kingdom was prepared from the foundation of the world, 
and God’s purpose is to make good His promise of a 
kingdom whereby His will shall be done on earth as it is 
in heaven. To Adam was the dominion given at the first. 
He failed, but the one of whom Adam was the figure and 
promise (Romans 5:14) “Shall not fail nor be discouraged 
until He hath set judgment in the earth.” (Isa. 42:4). 
Becoming alienated from the way of God and turning every 
one to his own way, all flesh became corrupt and filled the 
earth with violence. God swept away that world of the 
ungodly with a flood, and bringing Noah with his family 
by the ark into a new world condition, gave to them the 
dominion over it, and established government by the author- 
ity of heaven in the hand of man. Noah and his genera- 
tion failed, filling the measure of their transgression on 
the plains of Shinar. God thereupon called out of that 
rebellion Abram, and promised to make of him the great 
nation that should bless the earth. This nation was to be 
born in pain and brought forth with travail from the land 
of Egypt (a type of the Flesh) to walk in Canaan (walking 
in the Spirit). On their journey thither Balaam is hired 


— 389 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


to curse them, but can only bless and in blessing declares 
Israel to be the nation which has the King who shall have 
dominion. Under King Soloman Israel showed forth a 
faint glimmer of the heavenly kingdom that shall be, when 
it shall come to pass as it is written, “Arise, shine for thy 
‘light is come and the Glory of the Lord is risen upon 
thee.” But Soloman failed, and failure increased until the 
prophet Isaiah described the condition of the kingdom as 
“From the sole of the feet even unto the head there is no 
soundness in it; but wounds and bruises, and putrifying 
sores.” Jeremiah declares that for healing they shall go 
into captivity and be returned to power and pre-eminence 
after a period. Ezekiel saw the glory of the Lord depart 
from Israel rendering her desolate and naked. In that 
condition Daniel prayed for her, and requested God not to 
put off the restoration, nor defer it, but in that time to 
forgive and heal the wounds of the people. In response 
to Daniel’s prayer God sent His messenger to inform Daniel 
that the restoration was put off for seventy weeks of years. 
(This statement known to Bible readers as the prophecy 
of the seventy weeks has been so voluminously written 
about that any desiring to obtain information may do so 
by writing any Bible, tract or publishing house). In ac- 
cordance with that prophecy, John the Baptist came pro- 
claiming The kingdom of heaven at hand, inasmuch as 
the King, in whom is the power and the glory, was born 
in Bethlehem. The King came according to the words of 
the prophets, and those to whom He came refused Him 
saying, “We have no King but Caeser.” In the days of 
His rejection, the Lord Jesus Messiah spake to the people 
in the parable of the householder demanding fruit from 
his vineyard, in which He showed those who rejected Him, 


2300 


ee a ce 
GOD HATH SPOKEN 
E, a ee, 


that He knew, that they recognized Him as the heir to the 
throne of David, and knowing it, wilfully rebelled against 
Him. There was nothing left therefore but that He should 
come again and miserably destroy those wicked men. And 
to this agree the words of the prophets as it is written 
“After this”’—taking out of the world a people for His 
Name—“I will return.” 

After the church is caught up to meet the Lord in 
glory, conditions in the world will revert to the character 
of the days following the crucifixion. It will be with the 
world is if no Christian era had been known. This is 
understandable when we realize that the “truly born again” 
Christians are the salt of the earth. Imagine what the 
condition of life would be if there were no salt sea into 
which the sewage pollution of the cities might run, to be 
processed by the great ordinances of God’s good gov- 
ernance. God moves upon the corrupted waters through 
His sun and moon. The sun arises upon the waters with 
healing in his beams, draws them up to himself in purifying 
strength and letting them fall in power, they refresh the 
world. So the Lord has drawn believers to Himself—The 
Sun of Righteousness—and as living water they keep the 
great mass of men from becoming altogether corrupt. (John 
7 : 37-38.) Because of this, when the church is caught up 
to meet the returning Lord on His way, between the 
departure of the truly saved and their appearance with the 
Lord in power and glory (Rev. 1:7), all flesh will break 
out in open rebellion against God and His Christ. (Matt. 
24:37; Gen. 6:5.) This rebellion against the Lord at His 
return will unite the unbelieving Jews and Gentiles and 
fulfill the 2nd Psalm. The Calvary rebellion did not ex- 
haust the predictions of this Psalm, but the Armageddon 


— 391 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


rebellion evidently will. Thus it is written, “Why do the 
nations rage, and the people imagine a vain thing? The 
Kings of the earth set themselves and the rulers take 
counsel together against the Lord and against His Anointed 
saying: let us break their bands asunder and cast away 
their cords from us.” 

The figure of breaking the bands and casting away the 
cords is taken from the Tabernacle which God had Israel 
construct as His dwelling place among them. The boards 
which supplied the framework of that Tabernacle were 
held upright in sockets by cords or bands, which being 
secured by stakes outside and inside passed over the top 
of the board and being attached thereto upheld the con- 
taining limits of the structure. If these bands or cords 
were broken and cast away, the Tabernacle collapsed, and 
the dwelling place of God ruined. 

China some years ago set aside its imperial form of 
government and took up the republican. After a lapse 
of time, it was announced that China was about to restore 
its imperial form with the man who had been elected presi- 
dent from among the people, as the new Emperor. Imme- 
diately Japan uttered its protest. It was declared impossible 
that a man whose antecedents they well knew, could sit as 
Emperor of China on an equality with their own Emperor, 
whom they knew to be a son of the gods. 

It is reported that at the present Peace Conference, 
what should be done to the former Emperor of Germany 
was under discussion. Some thought he ought to be tried, 
condemned and made to suffer as a murderer; but the 
Japanese delegates protested such a course, on the ground, 
that as Emperor he ruled by divine right, and they could 
not suffer one in whom divinity dwelled to be treated as an

— 392 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


ordinary mortal. This is the clearest illustration possible 
of how the heathen remain faithful to their gods which 
are no gods. While the delegates from so-called Christian 
nations—nations which set apart days and nights and 
seasons on which their populations were urged to pray to 
the Lord Jesus Christ as the only true God, that He 
might have mercy upon them, delivering them from the 
scourge of war—agreed to not make mention of His Name 
nor make prayer to Him at that Peace Conference. What 
is the significance of this refusal to acknowledge the Lord 
Jesus Christ at the council of the nations, but the beginning 
of that process described as breaking God’s bands and 
casting away His cords from them. Surely “He that sit- 
teth in the Heavens doth laugh: the Lord shall have them 
in derision.” When the process is completed and the Kings 
of the earth have set themselves, and the rulers take counsel 
together without acknowledging Him in all their ways, 
then shall He speak unto them in His wrath and vex them 
in His sore displeasure; and to this agree the words of the 
prophets, as it is written “After this I will Return.”

FUTURE PUNISHMENT 


R. A. Torrey, D.D. 


INTRODUCTION 


The subject assigned me for this afternoon is Future 
Punishment. I shrink from speaking on that subject. I 
have a dread that approaches horror of speaking on that 
subject. I cannot tell you the pain I have in my heart 
every time I speak on that subject. I have lain on my face 
before God and sobbed as I have thought of what the 
Bible clearly teaches on the subject, and thought also of 
what it involves. It has seemed to me time and again that 
I could not have it so. I believe I would gladly die in 
agony and shame if thereby I could make it sure that 
all men would somewhere, sometime, somehow be brought 
to repentance and thus saved. To me the doctrine of 
Future Punishment is not a mere matter of speculative 
theory that I could discuss without emotion in cold intel- 
lectuality. I see it in its practical bearings on the destiny 
and the sufferings of the people I see around about me 
and thronging the streets. I see it and feel it in its rela- 
tions to real living men and women. I see it just as I see 
the recent sufferings of women and children in Belgium, 
Armenia and Korea. 

But as much as I shrink from speaking on the subject, 
I am glad to speak upon it. Indeed, I asked to speak upon 
it in preference to a subject upon which I was first re- 
quested to speak, but which I did not regard as funda- 
mental, and which has been left off the program. This 
subject is fundamental, it is vital, it is of immeasureable 


— 395 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


importance. A man’s stand upon this subject is decisive 
for his usefulness for God or his uselessness. It is at this 
point that more preachers and teachers who go seriously 
astray in their doctrine begin their descent than at almost 
any other point. When a preacher begins to wabble on 
Future Punishment, look out for him, he is very likely 
soon to go astray on the “Inspiration of the Bible,” and 
the “Inerrancy of the Bible,” then on the “Atonement by 
Shed Blood,” then on the “Virgin Birth of our Lord,” 
then on “His Literal Resurrection,” then on “His Deity,” 
and then - - - 


In a thirty years’ close study of men who were lead- 
ers in the church of Christ I have seen multitudes of men 
who were once a power for God shorn of their power for 
good by accepting Universalist, Restorationist, Conditional 
Immortality, Pastor Russellite and kindred views of future 
punishment. 


I. The Bible—the sole guide to the truth on this 
subject. 


The first thing I wish to say this afternoon is that, 
the Bible, is the sole guide to the truth on this subject. 
We know absolutely nothing about Future Punishment 
but what God has been pleased to tell us in this Book; 
just as we know absolutely nothing about the future 
blessedness of the saved except what God has been pleased 
to tell us in this Book. If you are truly logical and not 
merely sentimental, if you give up what the Bible teaches 
on the one subject you will give up what it teaches on 
the other. If a man will believe that part of the Bible 
that he desires to believe and rejects that part of the Bible 
that he does not desire to believe, in plain unvarnished 


SS SS 


— 396 — 


RE AR SS a a 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


=o ee e eee 


English he is a fool. If the Bible is not true, we have no 
conclusive proof that there is either a heaven or a hell. 
And if it is true about one, it is true also about the other. 
Some men may be able to believe what they want to be- 
lieve but to doubt or deny what they want to doubt or 
deny. I am not built that way. My wishes play no part 
in my decision, I haye to be governed by my intellect; but, 
of course, I know that a will surrendered to the truth and 
to God does more than anything else to clarify the intel- 
lect. 

So our whole inquiry will be, “What does the Bible 
Teach on This Subject?” Some people are always running 
off on to their reasonings and their speculations, but specu- 
lation on this subject is necessarily entirely vain. On such 
a subject as this one the ounce of God’s revelation is worth 
a thousand tons of man’s speculation. I sometimes show 
men what the Bible teaches on this subject and they say, 
“But how do you reconcile that with the love of God?” 
I reply, “How do you know God is love?” We owe that 
truth entirely to the Bible. If the Bible is not true we 
have no proof that God is love; and, if you reject what 
the Bible teaches about Future Punishment and are logical, 
you must also give up your belief that God is love, and 
your whole foundation for your universalistic and kindred 
hopes is gone. 

II. What the Bible teaches about Future Punishment. 


What does the Bible teach about Future Punishment? 

Of course, there is not time to go into this subject in full- 

ness in all its details, but we can set forth the tunaamental 
facts taught in the Bible. 

- 1. First then, the Bible teaches that as a result of 

sin, and especially of the crowning sin of rejecting the 


— 397 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Savior, there is to be after death an immeasurable suffer- 
ing for those who sin in this life, and do not repent of 
their sins and accept Christ. There is no need to dwell 
at length on that point. The old crude form of Univer- 
salism that no matter how a man lives in this life he enters 
at once into blessedness at death has largely disappeared, 
except from funeral sermons. If that were true, the kind- 
est thing that we could do for people in the slums and 
other unfortunates would be to put them to death at once 
in some painless way. But take one Bible statement and 
this statement gives the words of Jesus, Matt. 5:29, R.V., 
“And if thy right eye causeth thee to stumble, pluck it 
out, and cast it from thee: for it is profitable for thee 
that one of thy members should perish, and not thy whole 
body be cast into hell.” Certainly these words of our 
Lord mean that there is to be after death for those who 
sin and do not repent, such intense suffering that the 
greatest possible present calamity would be preferable 
to it. 


2. The second thing that the Bible teaches about 
Future Punishment is, that the body shall share with the 
soul in the suffering of the lost in the world to come. 
Take the verge that we have just quoted. In this Jesus 
Christ says, “the body”—and by the body he certainly 
means just what he says, “the body’—‘“shall be cast into 
hell.” Take another utterance of our Lord, Matt. 10:28, 
R.V., “And be not afraid of them that kill the body, but 
are not able to kill the soul: but rather fear him who is 
able to destroy both soul and body in hell.” Here to the 
most distinct and definite statement possible that the body 
as well as the soul is to suffer in the “destruction” of hell. 
Neither the blessed nor the lost are to exist in the world 


a a 


— 398 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


to come as disembodied spirits. There is to be a resur- 
rection of the just and the unjust. This our Lord defin- 
nitely declares in John 5:28, 29, R.V. He says, “the 
hour cometh, in which all that are in the tombs shall hear 
his voice, and shall come forth; they that have done good, 
unto the resurrection of life; and they that have done 
evil, unto the resurrection of judgment.” Resurrection 
has to do with the body and the body only. The spirit 
does not tumble down and decay and therefore needs no 
resurrection. The passage just quoted says, “all that are 
in the tombs.” What is “in the tombs’—the body, and 
the body only. The spirits of the lost at death go into 
Hades; the body (and the body only) into the tomb where 
it crumbles into dust. At the resurrection the body is 
raised and the spirit joins it. At death the spirits of the 
saved depart to be with Christ in conscious blessedness, 
which Paul says is very far better than the most blessed 
experience in the body in our present lives (Phil. 1: 21-23). 
The bodies of the blessed who pass away before our Lord 
returns crumble into dust. At the second coming of Christ 
the bodies of the blessed are raised and reunited with the 
redeemed spirits. The redeemed spirit hereafter at the 
second coming of Christ shall be clothed upon with a re- 
deemed body, fit partner of the redeemed spirit that in- 
habits it, and partaker with it in all of its joy; and the 
lost spirit shall be clothed upon with a lost body, fit part- 
ner of the lost spirit that inhabits it, and partaker with it 
in all its misery. While the bodily torments of hell are 
not the most important feature of Future Punishment, 
while the mental agony, the agony of remorse, the agony 
of shame, the agony of despair, is worse, immeasureably 
worse; nevertheless, bodily suffering, a bodily suffering in 


— 399 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


comparison with which no pain on earth is as anything, 
is a feature of Future Punishment. 


3. In the third place, the Bible teaches that the suf- 
ferings of the lost will be conscious, that the lost will not 
be annihilated or simply exist in non-conscious existence. 
This is the plain teaching of Luke 16: 19-31, the story of 
the rich man and Lazarus in the future world. All man- 
ner of allegorizing has been used in attempting to explain 
away these words of our Lord, but these allegorical ex- 
planations are simply ridiculous. The same thing is clearly 
taught in Rev. 14:9-11 compared with Rev. 20:10. In 
Rev. 14:9-11, R.V., we read, “If any man worshippeth 
the beast and his image, and receiveth a mark on his fore- 
head, or upon his hand, he also shall drink of the wine 
of the wrath of God, which is prepared unmixed in the cup 
of his anger; and he shall be tormented with fire and brim- 
stone in the presence of the holy angels, and in the pres- 
ence of the Lamb: And the smoke of their torment goeth 
up forever and ever: and they have no rest day and night, 
they that worship the beast and his image, and who so 
receiveth the mark of his name.” Now this certainly de- 
scribes conscious suffering of the intensest kind and cannot 
be fairly and honestly interpreted in any other way. In 
Rev. 20:10, R.V., we read, “And the devil that deceived 
them was cast into the lake of fire and brimstone, 
where are also the beast and the false prophet; and they 
shall be tormented day and night forever and ever.” These 
words unmistakably speak of conscious torment! We are 
told that they shall have no rest “day nor night,” which 
would be impossible language to use by any honest speaker 
or writer if the punishment were unconscious. 

4. The fourth thing that the Bible teaches about 


Soo 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Future Punishment is that the future destiny of the indi- 
vidual depends entirely upon what he does with Jesus 
Christ. One passage is sufficient to show that, though a 
multitude might be adduced. That passage is John 3: 36. 
“He that believeth on the Son hath eternal life; but he 
that believeth not the Son shall not see life; but the wrath 
of God abideth on him.” 


5. The fifth thing that the Bible teaches on this sub- 
ject is, that Future Punishment is endless. In Matt. 
25: 41-46, R.V., our Lord himself is recorded as saying, 
“Then shall he say also unto them on the left hand, Depart 
from me, ye cursed, into eternal fire which is prepared 
for the devil and his angels * * * and these (2. e. 
these on the left hand) shall go away into eternal punish- 
ment: but the righteous into eternal life.” It is often said 
that the word “aionios” used in these two verses does not 
by tts etymology necessarily imply endlessness. Even 
were we to admit, which we do not, that this were true, 
every scholar knows that it is one of the laws of the inter- 
pretation of any book that the meaning of words in any 
language or any book must be determined by usage. What 
is the usage in this case? This word is used seventy-two 
times in the New Testament. Forty-four of these seventy- 
two times it is used in the phrase “eternal life,” that “eter- 
nal life” is endless cannot be questioned. It is used fifteen 
times in connections where the idea of endlessness is abso- 
lutely necessary. This covers fifty-nine of the seventy-two 
instances in which the word is used. In the fifty-nine 
instances the thought of endlessness is absolutely neces- 
sary. In not a single one of the remaining thirteen cases 
is it used of anything that is known to end. If usage can 
determine anything, it determines to a demonstration that 


— 401 — 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


the usage of this word in the New Testament necessarily 
implies endlessness. But that is not all, the context as 
well as the usage demands that in this instance, in connec- 
tion with punishment, the word must imply endlessness. 
The context is this, “And these shall go away into eternal 
punishment: but the righteous into eternal life.” The same 
Greek word is used twice. As our Lord was at least an 
honest man he could not use the one word twice in the 
same sentence with a different meaning, and if that life 
into which the righteous go away is endless life, then the 
punishment into which the cursed go is endless also. This 
cannot be denied without questioning either the intelligence 
or honesty of the Lord Jesus. 


But even that is not all. We read in the passage 
Rev. 14:9-11, which we have already quoted, that the 
sufferings of the lost are “forever and ever,” and that 
throughout this “forever and ever” they “have no rest day 
nor night.” Here another Greek expression is used. There 
are two forms of this expression, one of them literally 
translated is “unto the ages of the ages,” the other is “unto 
ages of ages,” the only difference between the two being 
the omission of the article in the latter form. Now these 
expressions are used twelve times in the last book of the 
Bible. In eight of these twelve instances the expression 
refers to the duration of the existence, or reign, or glory 
of God or Christ. Once it is used of the duration of the 
blessed reign of the righteous. And in three remaining 
instances it is used of the duration of the torment of the 
devil, the beast, the false prophet and the impenitent. If 
we deal honestly with the words of our Lord Jesus Christ 
and of the inspired apostles, it is impossible to read the 
doctrine of endless conscious suffering of those who reject 


ST SE i SS RR RE i EEE SE ESS 


K 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


Christ out of the Bible. If anyone could produce me one 
single passage in the Bible that, fairly construed, according 
to its context and the usage of the words and grammatical 
construction that clearly taught that the punishment of the 
wicked would not be absolutely endless and that some- 
where, sometime, somehow all would repent and be saved, 
it would be the happiest day of my life. But no such pas- 
sage can be found. I have searched for it from the first 
chapter of Genesis to the last chapter of The Revelation 
but cannot find it, it is not there. I am thoroughly familiar 
with the passages that men urge. I have formerly used 
them myself, but they will not bear the construction that is 
put upon them if we deal honestly with them. 


6. In the sixth place, the Bible teaches that the ques- 
tion of our eternal destiny is settled this side of the grave. 
We read in 11 Cor. 5:10, R.V., “for we must all be made 
manifest before the judgment seat of Christ; that each 
one may receive the things done in the body, according to 
what he hath done, whether it be good or bad.” Now 
of course this has to do primarily with the judgment of 
believers, but it shows that the eternal judgment is deter- 
mined by what is done “in the body,” what is done this 
side the grave, what is done before we shall “shuffle off 
this mortal coil.” In Hebrews 9:27 we read, “it is ap- 
pointed unto men once to die, and after this cometh judg- 
ment.” The meaning of this is plain; namely, the eternal 
judgment is determined before death. But our Lord Jesus 
Himself says the decisive word, the word that would be 
decisive if it stood alone. In John 8:21, “I go away and 
ye shall seek me, and shall die in your sin: whither I go, 
ye cannot come.” Here our Lord says plainly that those 
who die in their sins cannot go where He does, that the 


= 403 == 


GOD HATH SPOKEN 


destinies of the future are settled in the life that now is, 
settled this side the grave. 


CoNCLUSION 


It is clear to anyone who will go to the Bible to find 
out what it teaches and not merely to read his own views 
into it, that the Bible does not hold out one ray of hope 
to any man who dies without having accepted Jesus Christ 
as Savior and surrendered to Him as his Lord and Master, 
confessed Him before the world in the life that now is. 
Many there are who undertake to do this. They are taking 
a terrible responsibility upon themselves, they dare to do 
what the divinely inspired authors of the Bible have not 
done. They lull men to sleep in sin and worldliness and 
inaction. What shall the harvest be?
